<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For each fixed $`0\lt \kappa\lt 8`$, let $`d=1+\kappa/8`$. The gauge $`h(r)=r^d(\log\log(1/r))^{(2-d)/2}`$ at sufficiently small radii almost surely gives positive finite Hausdorff measure to every nontrivial positive-time compact segment of chordal SLE<sub><i>κ</i></sub>. This gives an explicit solution to Schramm's Hausdorff-measure problem in this parameter range. The entire trace has finite expected measure in every bounded disk. The exponent-one iterated-logarithm gauge suggested by Schramm is not sigma-finite on any such segment.

- **thm-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $\gamma$ be ordinary chordal $\mathrm{SLE}_\kappa$ from $0$ to $\infty$ in $\mathbb H$, parametrized by half-plane capacity $2t$, and set $\Gamma=\gamma([0,\infty))$. For each fixed $0<\kappa<8$, on one event of probability one, $$0<\mathcal H^h(\gamma([s,t]))<\infty \qquad\text{for every real }0<s<t<\infty.$$ For every deterministic $0<R<\infty$, $$\mathbb E\mathcal H^h(\Gamma\cap\overline B(0,R)) \le C_{\kappa,R}<\infty.$$ In particular, on the same probability-one event, $$\mathcal H^h\bigl(\Gamma\cap([-m,m]+i[0,m])\bigr)<\infty \qquad(m=1,2,\ldots).$$
  - Explanation: The regular gauge gives positive finite measure on every positive-time compact segment, and finite expected whole-trace measure in bounded disks, for each fixed admissible parameter.
- **cor-larger-gauge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-larger-gauge)): Let $g(r)=r^d\log\log(1/r)$ at sufficiently small radii, with any continuous nondecreasing completion. On the event in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the restriction of $\mathcal H^g$ to every $\gamma([s,t])$, $0<s<t<\infty$, is not sigma-finite; in particular its total measure is infinite.
  - Explanation: The larger comparison gauge has non-sigma-finite measure on every stated segment.
- **geom-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-extension)): Let $\eta:[0,T]\to\overline{\mathbb H}$ be continuous, with $\eta(0)=0$, and let $D$ be the unbounded component of $\mathbb H\setminus\eta[0,T]$. Suppose $D$ is the surviving simply connected domain of the trace. Its conformal uniformization $F:\mathbb H\to D$ extends continuously from the closed half-plane in the spherical topology. Boundary parameters of $F$ specify prime ends; distinct parameters may have the same physical image.
  - Explanation: The surviving domain has a continuous spherical boundary uniformization; distinct prime ends may share a physical image.
- **geom-fresh-tip** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-fresh-tip)): In the preceding setting, suppose $a=\eta(T)\in\mathbb H\cap\partial D$ and $a\notin\eta[0,T)$. Then $F$ has exactly one boundary parameter with image $a$. For the Loewner uniformization this parameter is the current driving point. Every access in $D$ tending to $a$ approaches that same prime end.
  - Explanation: A fresh interior tip has a unique boundary parameter and prime end.
- **geom-first-hit-access** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-first-hit-access)): Let $\overline{B(z,r)}$ lie in the initial surviving domain, and write $\eta$ for the future trace. Let $\tau$ be its first time reaching $\partial B(z,r)$. Suppose $\tau<\infty$ and $z$ belongs to the surviving domain $D_\tau$. Then, writing $a=\eta(\tau)$, the segment $(a,z]$ is contained in $D_\tau$ and approaches the current tip prime end at $a$. If $g:D_\tau\to\mathbb H$ sends tip to $0$, target to infinity, and is scaled so that $\|g(z)\|=1$, the image of $[a,z]$ is a continuous path from $0$ to modulus $1$ whose inverse image is contained in $\overline{B(z,r)}$.
  - Explanation: A first disk hit with a surviving center supplies radial access to the current tip prime end.
- **geom-smooth-driver** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-smooth-driver)): Let $\beta$ be a compact regular smooth simple arc, starting at $0$, initially vertical, and otherwise contained in $\mathbb H$. Its successive prefixes, parametrized by half-plane capacity $2t$, are generated by a continuous chordal Loewner driver on a compact interval.
  - Explanation: A smooth simple slit has a continuous capacity-parametrized Loewner driver.
- **geom-slit-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-slit-support)): Let $\beta$ be as in Lemma <a href="#geom:smooth-driver" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:smooth-driver">6</a>, and let $q$ be its terminal point. Let $N$ be a relative open neighborhood of the whole slit in $\overline{\mathbb H}$, including a relative neighborhood of $0$, and let $V$ be an open neighborhood of $q$. Chordal $\mathrm{SLE}_{\kappa}$ satisfies $$\mathbb P\{\text{for some }t<\infty, \ \gamma[0,t]\subset N\text{ and }\gamma(t)\in V\}>0.$$ Suppose in addition that $G_1,\ldots,G_m$ are pairwise disjoint crosscuts of $N$, each separating $0$ from all later crosscuts and from $V\cap N$. On the displayed event, the first hits of $G_1,\ldots,G_m$ occur in that order. The probability asserted here is pointwise in the slit and tube; no uniform positive lower bound over different slits or pasts is claimed.
  - Explanation: A prescribed slit tube has positive passage probability, with ordered crosscuts, without uniformity across slits.
- **geom-restart-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-restart-support)): At a finite stopping time $\tau$ for ordinary chordal SLE in $\mathbb H$, fix a realized past for which the conditional continuation law $\mathbb P_\tau$ is defined. Let $D$ be its surviving domain, and let $F:\mathbb H\to D$ send $0$ to the current tip prime end and infinity to the target. Use its continuous spherical extension on $\overline{\mathbb H}$. Let $\alpha:[0,1]\to\overline{\mathbb H}$ be a continuous simple path with $\alpha(0)=0$ and $\alpha((0,1])\subset\mathbb H$. If $O$ is relatively open in $\overline D$ and contains $F(\alpha[0,1])$, and $V\subset D$ is an open neighborhood of $F(\alpha(1))$, then the restarted half-plane trace $\widetilde\gamma$ satisfies $$\mathbb P_\tau\{\text{for some }t<\infty, \ F(\widetilde\gamma[0,t])\subset O \text{ and }F(\widetilde\gamma(t))\in V\}>0.$$ The same statement holds for a Euclidean similarity of the evolution. The positive probability may depend on the realized past, $\alpha$, $O$, and $V$.
  - Explanation: Pointwise support persists after a finite stopped past and conformal mapping, with probability depending on the state.
- **ll-finite-tilt** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ll-finite-tilt)): For $0\le u<\infty$, let $\tau_u$ be the first time the live point has $R_t=R_0e^{-u}$, with value infinity if this level is never reached. Then $$\mathbb E\left[M_{\tau_u}(z) \mathbf1_{\{\tau_u<\infty\}}\right]=M_0(z).$$ The normalized weight defines a consistent law on the stopped path through $\tau_u$. Under that law, in radius time, $$d\theta_s=\frac{\sqrt\kappa}{2}\,dW_s +\cot\theta_s\,ds, \qquad \mathcal L^*=\frac\kappa8\partial_\theta^2 +\cot\theta\,\partial_\theta. \label{ll:tilt-generator}$$ Both endpoints are unattainable at finite radius time. These claims hold conditionally after any finite stopping time at which the point is live, with the same constants and the restarted conformal map.
  - Explanation: Finite radius stops define consistent centered laws with unattainable angular endpoints.
- **ll-inverse-angle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ll-inverse-angle)): For every $u_0>0$ there is $C_{u_0}<\infty$ such that $$\sup_{\theta\in(0,\pi)}\sup_{s\ge u_0} \mathbb E^*_{\theta}\sin^{-a_0}\theta_s\le C_{u_0}.$$ In particular, after one unit of radius time the probability that $\sin\theta$ exceeds a fixed positive constant is bounded below, uniformly over the starting angle.
  - Explanation: The centered angular diffusion has a uniform inverse-angle moment after positive radius time.
- **ll-approach** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ll-approach)): Let $0<r<R_0$ and freeze the density at the first live stop with $R=r$. Almost surely, on the event that the live radius never reaches $r$, the density has terminal value zero, whether its original Loewner-time lifetime is finite or infinite. Furthermore, for $u\ge1$, $$\mathbb P\{\tau_u<\infty\} \le C e^{-pu}\sin^{a_0}\theta_0. \label{ll:radius-approach}$$ For $0<\varepsilon\le\delta_0/(8\mathrm e)$, $$\mathbb P\{S_\varepsilon(z)<\infty\} \le C M_0(z)\varepsilon^p. \label{ll:euclidean-approach}$$ At this stop the density has the exact first moment $$\label{ll:clearance-first-moment} \mathbb E\!\left[M_{S_\varepsilon}(z) \mathbf1_{\{S_\varepsilon<\infty\}}\right]=M_0(z).$$ Under the centered law, $S_\varepsilon$ occurs no later than the finite radius stop with $R=\varepsilon$. All statements have their conditional restarted versions.
  - Explanation: Stopped densities have terminal zero without approach, power-law approach bounds, and an exact clearance first moment.
- **ll-finite-clock** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ll-finite-clock)): Under the consistent centered laws, $T^*(z)=\lim_{u\to\infty}\tau_u$ is finite almost surely.
  - Explanation: Consistent centered laws have almost surely finite limiting Loewner time.
- **ll-near-real** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ll-near-real)): There are $C,c>0$ such that ordinary chordal SLE in $\mathbb H$ satisfies $$\mathbb P\{\operatorname{dist}(v,\gamma)<\varepsilon\} \le C\varepsilon^c, \qquad \tfrac12\le\|v\|\le2,\quad v\in\overline{\mathbb H},\quad 0<\varepsilon<\tfrac1{16}.$$ The same estimate holds after a simultaneous spatial rescaling of the annulus and target radius. The trace in this statement includes all future times and its real boundary points.
  - Explanation: A small target in the stated annulus has uniformly polynomial approach probability, including real boundary points.
- **ll-rectangle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ll-rectangle)): Let $H>0$, let $Q$ be a rectangle of fixed bounded aspect ratio, and let $u\in W^{1,2}(Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$ satisfy $0\le u\le H$ and $u_Q\le H/2$. Write $E=\int_Q\|\nabla u\|^2$. For any random set $\mathcal X$ for which every dyadic level-$k$ subrectangle of $Q$ is hit with probability at most $C_02^{-\alpha k}$, $$\mathbb P\{\mathcal X\cap\{u=H\}\ne\varnothing\} \le C\frac E{H^2}\exp\left(-c\frac{H^2}{E}\right)$$ whenever $E\le c_0H^2$. The constants depend only on the aspect ratio, $C_0$, and $\alpha$. If $E=0$, the probability is zero.
  - Explanation: Small Dirichlet energy and dyadic hitting bounds control the probability of hitting the top level set.
- **ll-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ll-localization)): Let $F:\mathbb H\to\mathbb C$ be univalent and admit a continuous extension to $\overline{\mathbb H}$ with values in the sphere. Suppose a continuous path $\eta$ in $\overline{\mathbb H}$ starts at $0$, reaches modulus $1$, and satisfies $F(\eta)\subset\overline B(q,r)$, where $r>0$. There exist $b,c,C>0$ and $L_0>1$, depending only on $\kappa$, such that for every $L\ge L_0$, ordinary chordal SLE satisfies $$\mathbb P\left\{F(\gamma[0,\sigma_{L^b}]) \not\subset B(q,Lr)\right\} \le CL^{-c}, \qquad \sigma_A=\inf\{t:\|\gamma(t)\|=A\}.$$ If the modulus stop is not reached, use the entire future trace. All four constants are independent of $F,q,r$, and of the path $\eta$. Boundary excursions are included.
  - Explanation: A physical access path yields conformal localization uniformly over the univalent maps in the hypotheses.
- **ll-swallowing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ll-swallowing)): Suppose $4<\kappa<8$. For ordinary chordal SLE and every $z\in\mathbb H$ with $\|z\|=1$, let $T_z$ be its swallowing time. There are $C,c>0$ such that $$\mathbb P\{\sigma_A\le T_z\}\le CA^{-c},\qquad A\ge2,$$ uniformly over the angle of $z$.
  - Explanation: In the stated swallowing regime, an interior marked point is unlikely to survive until a large modulus excursion.
- **ll-disk-rule** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ll-disk-rule)): Suppose $4<\kappa<8$. There exist $C,c>0$ and $L_0\ge2$, depending only on $\kappa$, with the following property. Consider ordinary chordal SLE in $\mathbb H$, possibly after a finite stopping time with a past already drawn, or a fixed similarity of such an evolution. Suppose $\overline B(z,r)$ is strictly inside the current surviving domain. Let $\tau$ be the subsequent first hit of $\partial B(z,r)$. For $L\ge L_0$, on $\{\tau<\infty,\ z\in D_\tau\}$, set $$\sigma=\inf\{t\ge\tau:\|\gamma(t)-z\|\ge Lr\},$$ with value infinity if there is no such time. Then $$\mathbb P\{\sigma<\infty,\ z\in D_\sigma \mid\mathcal F_\tau\}\le CL^{-c}.$$ The event in the display is an exit while the point is still live. The constants are independent of the past, the initial disk, and the exit time.
  - Explanation: The conditional probability of leaving an enlarged physical disk while its center remains live decays polynomially.
- **mesh-tail** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mesh-tail)): There are constants $C,c>0$ and $0<\delta_0<\rho_{\rm col}$, depending only on $\kappa$ and the fixed collar, such that, for $0<r_f\le\delta\le\delta_0$, $$\mathbb P\left\{\int_A W_{r_f}(z)\,dA(z)>C\delta^{-p}\right\} \le C\exp(-c\delta^{-2}). \label{mesh:tail-equation}$$ The constants are uniform over the stated initial marked domains, including conditioning on any past for which the collar is free. They do not depend on the final distance $r_f$ or on an upper bound for the distance to the tip. The same estimate holds if only approaches by a fixed or stopping time are counted.
  - Explanation: Spatially integrated stopped densities obey a tail bound uniform in the final mesh and admissible collared past.
- **mesh-layer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mesh-layer)): Let $D$ be a simply connected plane domain with connected unbounded complement, and let $K_D$ be its Dirichlet Green function in the normalization $$K_{\mathbb H}(z,w)=\log\left\|\frac{z-\overline w}{z-w}\right\|.$$ For every $r>0$ and every $z\in D$, $$\int_{\{w\in D:\operatorname{dist}(w,\partial D)\le r\}} K_D(z,w)\,dA(w)\le Cr^2. \label{mesh:layer-bound}$$
  - Explanation: The Green occupation integral over a boundary layer is uniformly quadratic in the layer width.
- **win-concentration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-win-concentration)): Let $D\subset B(0,1)$ be Borel, let $0<r\le R$ and $K>0$ with $3R\le1$, and let $\gamma$ be a continuous adapted curve. For each $z\in D$, suppose that a test starts at a stopping time $\sigma_z$, with $\sigma_z=\infty$ if it never starts. At a finite start, $$\|\gamma(\sigma_z)-z\|=r.$$ Suppose its ending time $\tau_z\ge\sigma_z$ is a stopping time and $\gamma(t)\in B(z,R)$ for $\sigma_z\le t<\tau_z$. Let $Y_z$ be an $\mathcal F_{\tau_z}$-measurable output satisfying $0\le Y_z\le K$, and let $\mu_z\ge0$ be $\mathcal F_{\sigma_z}$-measurable, with $$\mathbb E[Y_z\mid\mathcal F_{\sigma_z}]\le\mu_z \quad\hbox{on }\{\sigma_z<\infty\}.$$ Assume the outputs and upper means are jointly measurable in $(z,\omega)$, are zero when there is no start, and are uniformly bounded. If a started window never ends, assume its output is zero. Then $$\mathbb E\exp\left(\lambda\int_D(Y_z-\mu_z)\,dA(z)\right) \le \exp(CK^2\lambda^2 R^2),\qquad \lambda\ge0, \label{win:mgf}$$ and hence, for $b>0$, $$\mathbb P\left\{\int_D(Y_z-\mu_z)\,dA(z)>b\right\} \le \exp\left(-\frac{c b^2}{K^2R^2}\right). \label{win:tail}$$ Here $C,c>0$ are absolute constants. The same conclusion holds conditionally on an initial sigma field if all the hypotheses and conditional-mean estimates hold after that conditioning.
  - Explanation: Adaptively ordered spatial windows obey exponential concentration without an independence assumption.
- **retain-sausage** (theorem, [source](#stmt-retain-sausage)): For every deterministic $J=[s,t]$ with $0\le s<t<\infty$, $$\lim_{a\downarrow0}\limsup_{e\downarrow0} \mathbb P\{X_e(J)<a\}=0. \label{retain:sausage-tail}$$ Equivalently, for every $\varepsilon>0$ there are $a,e_0>0$ such that $\mathbb P\{X_e(J)<a\}<\varepsilon$ for all $0<e<e_0$. The same conclusion can be required simultaneously for any fixed finite list of such intervals, with total exceptional probability as small as desired.
  - Explanation: On deterministic nontrivial time intervals, normalized sausage mass has vanishing small-value probability in the stated iterated limit.
- **rc-step** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rc-step)): There are $q,c,v_0>0$, depending only on $\kappa$ and the fixed ribbon constants, with the following property. For every stopped past satisfying the preceding ribbon conditions and every deterministic $0<v<v_0$, the conditional probability is at least $q$ that the next first crossing of $s=k+1$ occurs in finite time, with tip offset $\|x\|<\epsilon/4$, while the trace segment $\gamma_{\rm step}$ from the current front to that next crossing stays in $$k-1/4<s<k+1,\qquad \|x\|<\epsilon,$$ before that crossing, and that $$\label{rc:step-mass} v^{-p}\operatorname{Area} \left(N_v(\gamma_{\rm step})\cap P((k+1/3,k+2/3)\times(-\epsilon,\epsilon))\right)\ge c.$$ All three constants are uniform over the ribbon and the entire stopped past. For a ribbon of half-width $\ell$, the same assertion has mesh cutoff $v_0\ell$ and lower bound $c\ell^d$ in <a href="#rc:step-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rc:step-mass">[rc:step-mass]</a>.
  - Explanation: Every admissible ribbon past permits a uniformly positive conditional passage-and-mass probability at each sufficiently fine deterministic mesh.
- **es-guide-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-es-guide-compactness)): Let $F_n:\mathbb H\to D_n$ be conformal, with inverse $g_n$, and let $\alpha_n:[0,T]\to\overline{D_n}$ be $C$-Lipschitz guides satisfying $$\begin{gathered} \alpha_n(0)=a_n\in\partial D_n,\qquad \alpha_n(t)\in D_n\quad(t>0),\\ \operatorname{dist}(\alpha_n(t),\partial D_n) \ge c\min(t,1). \end{gathered}$$ Assume that $a_n$ stays in a bounded set and that $\beta_n=g_n\circ\alpha_n$, initially defined for $t>0$, extends continuously to $\beta_n(0)=0$. Fix $t_w\in(0,T]$, put $w_n=\alpha_n(t_w)$, and suppose that $$\|g_n(w_n)\|=1,\qquad \operatorname{Im}g_n(w_n)\ge s>0.$$ There is a subsequence on which $\alpha_n$ converges uniformly to a guide $\alpha$, $F_n$ converges locally uniformly on $\mathbb H$ to a nonconstant univalent map $F$, and $\beta_n$ converges locally uniformly on $(0,T]$ to $\beta$, where $F\circ\beta=\alpha$. In addition, $$\label{es:uniform-tip-collapse} \lim_{h\downarrow0}\sup_n\sup_{0\le t\le h}\|\beta_n(t)\|=0.$$ Consequently $\beta(0+)=0$. If $a_n\to a$, then $a\notin F(\mathbb H)$ and $F(\beta(t))\to a$ as $t\downarrow0$.
  - Explanation: Normalized conformal maps and cleared access guides admit nondegenerate subsequential limits, with uniform tip collapse along the guides.
- **es-good-semicircle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-es-good-semicircle)): Let $F$ be univalent on $\mathbb H$, and let $a\notin F(\mathbb H)$. Suppose $\beta:[0,T]\to\overline{\mathbb H}$ is continuous, $\beta(0)=0$, $\beta(t)\in\mathbb H$ for $t>0$, and $F(\beta(t))\to a$ as $t\downarrow0$. Fix $r>0$ and $L>1$, and write $A=\log L$. Choose $t_0>0$ so that $$\|F(\beta(t))-a\|\le r/L\quad(0<t\le t_0), \qquad b_0=\|\beta(t_0)\|>0.$$ In every unit logarithmic slab $(q,q+1)$ lying below $\log b_0$, there is $B\in(e^q,e^{q+1})$ such that, provided $A>2\pi^2$, $$\label{es:semicircle-bound} \sup_{0<\theta<\pi}\|F(Be^{i\theta})-a\| \le r\exp\{-A+\pi\sqrt{2A}\}.$$ Moreover $$\label{es:sector-bound} \sup_{\substack{B/8\le\|z\|\le B\\ \operatorname{Im}z\ge \|z\|/2}} \|F(z)-a\| \le r\exp\{-A+\pi\sqrt{2A}+6\}.$$ For $A\ge64$ both upper bounds are smaller than $r/4$.
  - Explanation: A logarithmic energy argument supplies a small image semicircle and a controlled central sector.
- **rc-dense** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rc-dense)): Fix $s_0>0$. There are constants $c,C,C_2>0$, $C_1>1$, and an integer $m_0$ with the following property. Let $S$ be a stopping time of ordinary chordal SLE in $\mathbb H$, and let $T_z$ be the lifetime of an interior marked point $z$. The assertion below holds conditionally almost surely on $\{S<\infty,\ S<T_z\}$, in the surviving state at $S$ or its image under a translation and positive dilation, with the transformed marked point still denoted by $z$. Suppose the marked point in that state has conformal radius $r$ and angle sine at least $s_0$. Let $\rho$ be the first subsequent live stop at which its conformal radius is $r/C_1$, with $\rho=\infty$ if that level is not reached, and use the finite centered law of Lemma <a href="#ll:finite-tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ll:finite-tilt">9</a> through $\rho$. For each integer $m\ge m_0$ there is a deterministic $\delta_m>0$, independent of the stopped state, such that, for every prescribed deterministic physical mesh $0<v\le r\delta_m$, there is an $\mathcal F_\rho$-measurable success event with conditional centered probability at least $$\label{rc:dense-probability} c\exp(-Cm^2)$$ on which the experiment ends at a finite stopping time $T<\rho$ and generates sausage mass at least $$\label{rc:dense-mass} v^{-p}\operatorname{Area} \bigl(N_v(\gamma_{\rm experiment})\cap B(z,C_2r)\bigr) \ge c r^d m^p.$$
  - Explanation: A dense visit creates enhanced sausage mass with a quantitative probability and a deterministic state-uniform mesh cutoff.
- **lem-cover-charge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cover-charge)): For every $0<R<\infty$, there is $C_R<\infty$, depending only on $R$ and $\kappa$, such that $$\sup_{0<v\le1}\mathbb E\mu^v(B(0,R))\le C_R.$$
  - Explanation: The expected whole-trace normalized sausage measure in a bounded disk is uniformly bounded over the stated meshes.
- **prop-dense-cover-upper** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-dense-cover-upper)): For every $0<R<\infty$, $$\mathbb E\mathcal H^h \bigl(\Gamma\cap\overline B(0,R)\bigr)\le C_R<\infty. \label{eq:cover-all-time-expectation}$$ Consequently, on one probability-one event, $$\mathcal H^h\bigl(\Gamma\cap([-m,m]+i[0,m])\bigr)<\infty \qquad(m=1,2,\ldots).$$ On that event every compact capacity-time segment has finite $\mathcal H^h$-measure.
  - Explanation: A single-mesh charging cover gives finite expected whole-trace gauge measure in bounded disks and simultaneous compact-segment finiteness.

If $g:D_\\tau\\to\\mathbb H$ sends tip to $0$, target to infinity, and is scaled so that $|g(z)|=1$, the image of $[a,z]$ is a continuous path from $0$ to modulus $1$ whose inverse image is contained in $\\overline{B(z,r)}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-230.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-geom-first-hit-access"
    },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-slit-support",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\beta$ be as in Lemma <a href=\"#geom:smooth-driver\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:smooth-driver\">6</a>, and let $q$ be its terminal point. Let $N$ be a relative open neighborhood of the whole slit in $\\overline{\\mathbb H}$, including a relative neighborhood of $0$, and let $V$ be an open neighborhood of $q$. Chordal $\\mathrm{SLE}_{\\kappa}$ satisfies $$\\mathbb P\\{\\text{for some }t<\\infty,\n       \\ \\gamma[0,t]\\subset N\\text{ and }\\gamma(t)\\in V\\}>0.$$ Suppose in addition that $G_1,\\ldots,G_m$ are pairwise disjoint crosscuts of $N$, each separating $0$ from all later crosscuts and from $V\\cap N$. On the displayed event, the first hits of $G_1,\\ldots,G_m$ occur in that order. The probability asserted here is pointwise in the slit and tube; no uniform positive lower bound over different slits or pasts is claimed.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-restart-support",
        "exact_quote": "At a finite stopping time $\\tau$ for ordinary chordal SLE in $\\mathbb H$, fix a realized past for which the conditional continuation law $\\mathbb P_\\tau$ is defined. Let $D$ be its surviving domain, and let $F:\\mathbb H\\to D$ send $0$ to the current tip prime end and infinity to the target. Use its continuous spherical extension on $\\overline{\\mathbb H}$. Let $\\alpha:[0,1]\\to\\overline{\\mathbb H}$ be a continuous simple path with $\\alpha(0)=0$ and $\\alpha((0,1])\\subset\\mathbb H$. If $O$ is relatively open in $\\overline D$ and contains $F(\\alpha[0,1])$, and $V\\subset D$ is an open neighborhood of $F(\\alpha(1))$, then the restarted half-plane trace $\\widetilde\\gamma$ satisfies $$\\mathbb P_\\tau\\{\\text{for some }t<\\infty,\n   \\ F(\\widetilde\\gamma[0,t])\\subset O\n       \\text{ and }F(\\widetilde\\gamma(t))\\in V\\}>0.$$ The same statement holds for a Euclidean similarity of the evolution. The positive probability may depend on the realized past, $\\alpha$, $O$, and $V$.",
        "source_id": "PM-230.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-ll-finite-tilt",
        "exact_quote": "For $0\\le u<\\infty$, let $\\tau_u$ be the first time the live point has $R_t=R_0e^{-u}$, with value infinity if this level is never reached. Then $$\\mathbb E\\left[M_{\\tau_u}(z)\n                   \\mathbf1_{\\{\\tau_u<\\infty\\}}\\right]=M_0(z).$$ The normalized weight defines a consistent law on the stopped path through $\\tau_u$. Under that law, in radius time, $$d\\theta_s=\\frac{\\sqrt\\kappa}{2}\\,dW_s\n                    +\\cot\\theta_s\\,ds,\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal L^*=\\frac\\kappa8\\partial_\\theta^2\n                    +\\cot\\theta\\,\\partial_\\theta.\n \\label{ll:tilt-generator}$$ Both endpoints are unattainable at finite radius time.       },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ll-approach",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0<r<R_0$ and freeze the density at the first live stop with $R=r$. Furthermore, for $u\\ge1$, $$\\mathbb P\\{\\tau_u<\\infty\\}\n \\le C e^{-pu}\\sin^{a_0}\\theta_0.\n \\label{ll:radius-approach}$$ For $0<\\varepsilon\\le\\delta_0/(8\\mathrm e)$, $$\\mathbb P\\{S_\\varepsilon(z)<\\infty\\}\n       \\le C M_0(z)\\varepsilon^p.\n \\label{ll:euclidean-approach}$$ At this stop the density has the exact first moment $$\\label{ll:clearance-first-moment}\n \\mathbb E\\!\\left[M_{S_\\varepsilon}(z)\n          \\mathbf1_{\\{S_\\varepsilon<\\infty\\}}\\right]=M_0(z).$$ Under the centered law, $S_\\varepsilon$ occurs no later than the finite radius stop with $R=\\varepsilon$.       },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ll-near-real",
        "exact_quote": "There are $C,c>0$ such that ordinary chordal SLE in $\\mathbb H$ satisfies $$\\mathbb P\\{\\operatorname{dist}(v,\\gamma)<\\varepsilon\\}\n       \\le C\\varepsilon^c,\n \\qquad \\tfrac12\\le|v|\\le2,\\quad\n v\\in\\overline{\\mathbb H},\\quad 0<\\varepsilon<\\tfrac1{16}.$$ The same estimate holds after a simultaneous spatial rescaling of the annulus and target radius. The trace in this statement includes all future times and its real boundary points.",
        "source_id": "PM-230.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ll-near-real",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ll-rectangle",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H>0$, let $Q$ be a rectangle of fixed bounded aspect ratio, and let $u\\in W^{1,2}(Q)\\cap C(\\overline Q)$ satisfy $0\\le u\\le H$ and $u_Q\\le H/2$. Write $E=\\int_Q|\\nabla u|^2$. For any random set $\\mathcal X$ for which every dyadic level-$k$ subrectangle of $Q$ is hit with probability at most $C_02^{-\\alpha k}$, $$\\mathbb P\\{\\mathcal X\\cap\\{u=H\\}\\ne\\varnothing\\}\n \\le C\\frac E{H^2}\\exp\\left(-c\\frac{H^2}{E}\\right)$$ whenever $E\\le c_0H^2$. The constants depend only on the aspect ratio, $C_0$, and $\\alpha$. If $E=0$, the probability is zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-230.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ll-localization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F:\\mathbb H\\to\\mathbb C$ be univalent and admit a continuous extension to $\\overline{\\mathbb H}$ with values in the sphere. Suppose a continuous path $\\eta$ in $\\overline{\\mathbb H}$ starts at $0$, reaches modulus $1$, and satisfies $F(\\eta)\\subset\\overline B(q,r)$, where $r>0$. There exist $b,c,C>0$ and $L_0>1$, depending only on $\\kappa$, such that for every $L\\ge L_0$, ordinary chordal SLE satisfies $$\\mathbb P\\left\\{F(\\gamma[0,\\sigma_{L^b}])\n                      \\not\\subset B(q,Lr)\\right\\}\n       \\le CL^{-c},\n \\qquad\n \\sigma_A=\\inf\\{t:|\\gamma(t)|=A\\}.$$ If the modulus stop is not reached, use the entire future trace. All four constants are independent of $F,q,r$, and of the path $\\eta$. Boundary excursions are included.",
        "source_id": "PM-230.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ll-localization",

All four constants are independent of $F,q,r$, and of the path $\\eta$. Boundary excursions are included.",
          "source_id": "PM-230.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ll-swallowing",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $4<\\kappa<8$. For ordinary chordal SLE and every $z\\in\\mathbb H$ with $|z|=1$, let $T_z$ be its swallowing time. There are $C,c>0$ such that $$\\mathbb P\\{\\sigma_A\\le T_z\\}\\le CA^{-c},\\qquad A\\ge2,$$ uniformly over the angle of $z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-230.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ll-swallowing",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $4<\\kappa<8$. For ordinary chordal SLE and every $z\\in\\mathbb H$ with $|z|=1$, let $T_z$ be its swallowing time. There are $C,c>0$ such that $$\\mathbb P\\{\\sigma_A\\le T_z\\}\\le CA^{-c},\\qquad A\\ge2,$$ uniformly over the angle of $z$.",
          "source_id": "PM-230.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose $4<\\kappa<8$. For ordinary chordal SLE and every $z\\in\\mathbb H$ with $|z|=1$, let $T_z$ be its swallowing time. There are $C,c>0$ such that $$\\mathbb P\\{\\sigma_A\\le T_z\\}\\le CA^{-c},\\qquad A\\ge2,$$ uniformly over the angle of $z$.",
      "explanation": "In the stated swallowing regime, an interior marked point is unlikely to survive until a large modulus excursion.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ll-disk-rule",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $4<\\kappa<8$. There exist $C,c>0$ and $L_0\\ge2$, depending only on $\\kappa$, with the following property. Consider ordinary chordal SLE in $\\mathbb H$, possibly after a finite stopping time with a past already drawn, or a fixed similarity of such an evolution. Suppose $\\overline B(z,r)$ is strictly inside the current surviving domain. Let $\\tau$ be the subsequent first hit of $\\partial B(z,r)$. For $L\\ge L_0$, on $\\{\\tau<\\infty,\\ z\\in D_\\tau\\}$, set $$\\sigma=\\inf\\{t\\ge\\tau:|\\gamma(t)-z|\\ge Lr\\},$$ with value infinity if there is no such time. Then $$\\mathbb P\\{\\sigma<\\infty,\\ z\\in D_\\sigma\n                    \\mid\\mathcal F_\\tau\\}\\le CL^{-c}.$$ The event in the display is an exit while the point is still live. The constants are independent of the past, the initial disk, and the exit time.",
        "source_id": "PM-230.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ll-disk-rule",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $4<\\kappa<8$. There exist $C,c>0$ and $L_0\\ge2$, depending only on $\\kappa$, with the following property. Consider ordinary chordal SLE in $\\mathbb H$, possibly after a finite stopping time with a past already drawn, or a fixed similarity of such an evolution. Suppose $\\overline B(z,r)$ is strictly inside the current surviving domain. Let $\\tau$ be the subsequent first hit of $\\partial B(z,r)$. For $L\\ge L_0$, on $\\{\\tau<\\infty,\\ z\\in D_\\tau\\}$, set $$\\sigma=\\inf\\{t\\ge\\tau:|\\gamma(t)-z|\\ge Lr\\},$$ with value infinity if there is no such time. Then $$\\mathbb P\\{\\sigma<\\infty,\\ z\\in D_\\sigma\n                    \\mid\\mathcal F_\\tau\\}\\le CL^{-c}.$$ The event in the display is an exit while the point is still live. The constants are independent of the past, the initial disk, and the exit time.",
          "source_id": "PM-230.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose $4<\\kappa<8$. There exist $C,c>0$ and $L_0\\ge2$, depending only on $\\kappa$, with the following property. Consider ordinary chordal SLE in $\\mathbb H$, possibly after a finite stopping time with a past already drawn, or a fixed similarity of such an evolution. Suppose $\\overline B(z,r)$ is strictly inside the current surviving domain. Let $\\tau$ be the subsequent first hit of $\\partial B(z,r)$. For $L\\ge L_0$, on $\\{\\tau<\\infty,\\ z\\in D_\\tau\\}$, set $$\\sigma=\\inf\\{t\\ge\\tau:|\\gamma(t)-z|\\ge Lr\\},$$ with value infinity if there is no such time. Then $$\\mathbb P\\{\\sigma<\\infty,\\ z\\in D_\\sigma\n                    \\mid\\mathcal F_\\tau\\}\\le CL^{-c}.$$ The event in the display is an exit while the point is still live. The constants are independent of the past, the initial disk, and the exit time.",
      "explanation": "The conditional probability of leaving an enlarged physical disk while its center remains live decays polynomially.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

      "id": "ll-disk-rule",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ll-disk-rule",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $4<\\kappa<8$. There exist $C,c>0$ and $L_0\\ge2$, depending only on $\\kappa$, with the following property. Consider ordinary chordal SLE in $\\mathbb H$, possibly after a finite stopping time with a past already drawn, or a fixed similarity of such an evolution. Suppose $\\overline B(z,r)$ is strictly inside the current surviving domain. Let $\\tau$ be the subsequent first hit of $\\partial B(z,r)$. For $L\\ge L_0$, on $\\{\\tau<\\infty,\\ z\\in D_\\tau\\}$, set $$\\sigma=\\inf\\{t\\ge\\tau:|\\gamma(t)-z|\\ge Lr\\},$$ with value infinity if there is no such time. Then $$\\mathbb P\\{\\sigma<\\infty,\\ z\\in D_\\sigma\n                    \\mid\\mathcal F_\\tau\\}\\le CL^{-c}.$$ The event in the display is an exit while the point is still live.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mesh-tail",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $C,c>0$ and $0<\\delta_0<\\rho_{\\rm col}$, depending only on $\\kappa$ and the fixed collar, such that, for $0<r_f\\le\\delta\\le\\delta_0$, $$\\mathbb P\\left\\{\\int_A W_{r_f}(z)\\,dA(z)>C\\delta^{-p}\\right\\}\n \\le C\\exp(-c\\delta^{-2}).\n \\label{mesh:tail-equation}$$ The constants are uniform over the stated initial marked domains, including conditioning on any past for which the collar is free. They do not depend on the final distance $r_f$ or on an upper bound for the distance to the tip. The same estimate holds if only approaches by a fixed or stopping time are counted.",
        "source_id": "PM-230.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

They do not depend on the final distance $r_f$ or on an upper bound for the distance to the tip. The same estimate holds if only approaches by a fixed or stopping time are counted.",
          "source_id": "PM-230.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mesh-layer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a simply connected plane domain with connected unbounded complement, and let $K_D$ be its Dirichlet Green function in the normalization $$K_{\\mathbb H}(z,w)=\\log\\left|\\frac{z-\\overline w}{z-w}\\right|.$$ For every $r>0$ and every $z\\in D$, $$\\int_{\\{w\\in D:\\operatorname{dist}(w,\\partial D)\\le r\\}}\n K_D(z,w)\\,dA(w)\\le Cr^2.\n \\label{mesh:layer-bound}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-230.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mesh-layer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a simply connected plane domain with connected unbounded complement, and let $K_D$ be its Dirichlet Green function in the normalization $$K_{\\mathbb H}(z,w)=\\log\\left|\\frac{z-\\overline w}{z-w}\\right|.$$ For every $r>0$ and every $z\\in D$, $$\\int_{\\{w\\in D:\\operatorname{dist}(w,\\partial D)\\le r\\}}\n K_D(z,w)\\,dA(w)\\le Cr^2.\n \\label{mesh:layer-bound}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-230.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $D$ be a simply connected plane domain with connected unbounded complement, and let $K_D$ be its Dirichlet Green function in the normalization $$K_{\\mathbb H}(z,w)=\\log\\left|\\frac{z-\\overline w}{z-w}\\right|.$$ For every $r>0$ and every $z\\in D$, $$\\int_{\\{w\\in D:\\operatorname{dist}(w,\\partial D)\\le r\\}}\n K_D(z,w)\\,dA(w)\\le Cr^2.\n \\label{mesh:layer-bound}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-win-concentration",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D\\subset B(0,1)$ be Borel, let $0<r\\le R$ and $K>0$ with $3R\\le1$, and let $\\gamma$ be a continuous adapted curve. For each $z\\in D$, suppose that a test starts at a stopping time $\\sigma_z$, with $\\sigma_z=\\infty$ if it never starts. At a finite start, $$|\\gamma(\\sigma_z)-z|=r.$$ Suppose its ending time $\\tau_z\\ge\\sigma_z$ is a stopping time and $\\gamma(t)\\in B(z,R)$ for $\\sigma_z\\le t<\\tau_z$. Let $Y_z$ be an $\\mathcal F_{\\tau_z}$-measurable output satisfying $0\\le Y_z\\le K$, and let $\\mu_z\\ge0$ be $\\mathcal F_{\\sigma_z}$-measurable, with $$\\mathbb E[Y_z\\mid\\mathcal F_{\\sigma_z}]\\le\\mu_z\n \\quad\\hbox{on }\\{\\sigma_z<\\infty\\}.$$ Assume the outputs and upper means are jointly measurable in $(z,\\omega)$, are zero when there is no start, and are uniformly bounded. If a started window never ends, assume its output is zero. Then $$\\mathbb E\\exp\\left(\\lambda\\int_D(Y_z-\\mu_z)\\,dA(z)\\right)\n       \\le \\exp(CK^2\\lambda^2 R^2),\\qquad \\lambda\\ge0,\n \\label{win:mgf}$$ and hence, for $b>0$, $$\\mathbb P\\left\\{\\int_D(Y_z-\\mu_z)\\,dA(z)>b\\right\\}\n       \\le \\exp\\left(-\\frac{c b^2}{K^2R^2}\\right).\n \\label{win:tail}$$ Here $C,c>0$ are absolute constants. The same conclusion holds conditionally on an initial sigma field if all the hypotheses and conditional-mean estimates hold after that conditioning.",
        "source_id": "PM-230.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-win-concentration",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D\\subset B(0,1)$ be Borel, let $0<r\\le R$ and $K>0$ with $3R\\le1$, and let $\\gamma$ be a continuous adapted curve. For each $z\\in D$, suppose that a test starts at a stopping time $\\sigma_z$, with $\\sigma_z=\\infty$ if it never starts. At a finite start, $$|\\gamma(\\sigma_z)-z|=r.$$ Suppose its ending time $\\tau_z\\ge\\sigma_z$ is a stopping time and $\\gamma(t)\\in B(z,R)$ for $\\sigma_z\\le t<\\tau_z$. Let $Y_z$ be an $\\mathcal F_{\\tau_z}$-measurable output satisfying $0\\le Y_z\\le K$, and let $\\mu_z\\ge0$ be $\\mathcal F_{\\sigma_z}$-measurable, with $$\\mathbb E[Y_z\\mid\\mathcal F_{\\sigma_z}]\\le\\mu_z\n \\quad\\hbox{on }\\{\\sigma_z<\\infty\\}.$$ Assume the outputs and upper means are jointly measurable in $(z,\\omega)$, are zero when there is no start, and are uniformly bounded. If a started window never ends, assume its output is zero. Then $$\\mathbb E\\exp\\left(\\lambda\\int_D(Y_z-\\mu_z)\\,dA(z)\\right)\n       \\le \\exp(CK^2\\lambda^2 R^2),\\qquad \\lambda\\ge0,\n \\label{win:mgf}$$ and hence, for $b>0$, $$\\mathbb P\\left\\{\\int_D(Y_z-\\mu_z)\\,dA(z)>b\\right\\}\n       \\le \\exp\\left(-\\frac{c b^2}{K^2R^2}\\right).\n \\label{win:tail}$$ Here $C,c>0$ are absolute constants. The same conclusion holds conditionally on an initial sigma field if all the hypotheses and conditional-mean estimates hold after that conditioning.",
          "source_id": "PM-230.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $D\\subset B(0,1)$ be Borel, let $0<r\\le R$ and $K>0$ with $3R\\le1$, and let $\\gamma$ be a continuous adapted curve. If a started window never ends, assume its output is zero. Then $$\\mathbb E\\exp\\left(\\lambda\\int_D(Y_z-\\mu_z)\\,dA(z)\\right)\n       \\le \\exp(CK^2\\lambda^2 R^2),\\qquad \\lambda\\ge0,\n \\label{win:mgf}$$ and hence, for $b>0$, $$\\mathbb P\\left\\{\\int_D(Y_z-\\mu_z)\\,dA(z)>b\\right\\}\n       \\le \\exp\\left(-\\frac{c b^2}{K^2R^2}\\right).\n \\label{win:tail}$$ Here $C,c>0$ are absolute constants. The same conclusion holds conditionally on an initial sigma field if all the hypotheses and conditional-mean estimates hold after that conditioning.",

        "anchor": "stmt-retain-sausage",
        "exact_quote": "For every deterministic $J=[s,t]$ with $0\\le s<t<\\infty$, $$\\lim_{a\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{e\\downarrow0}\n \\mathbb P\\{X_e(J)<a\\}=0.\n \\label{retain:sausage-tail}$$ Equivalently, for every $\\varepsilon>0$ there are $a,e_0>0$ such that $\\mathbb P\\{X_e(J)<a\\}<\\varepsilon$ for all $0<e<e_0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rc-step",
        "exact_quote": "There are $q,c,v_0>0$, depending only on $\\kappa$ and the fixed ribbon constants, with the following property. For every stopped past satisfying the preceding ribbon conditions and every deterministic $0<v<v_0$, the conditional probability is at least $q$ that the next first crossing of $s=k+1$ occurs in finite time, with tip offset $|x|<\\epsilon/4$, while the trace segment $\\gamma_{\\rm step}$ from the current front to that next crossing stays in $$k-1/4<s<k+1,\\qquad |x|<\\epsilon,$$ before that crossing, and that $$\\label{rc:step-mass}\n v^{-p}\\operatorname{Area}\n \\left(N_v(\\gamma_{\\rm step})\\cap\n   P((k+1/3,k+2/3)\\times(-\\epsilon,\\epsilon))\\right)\\ge c.$$ All three constants are uniform over the ribbon and the entire stopped past. For a ribbon of half-width $\\ell$, the same assertion has mesh cutoff $v_0\\ell$ and lower bound $c\\ell^d$ in <a href=\"#rc:step-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rc:step-mass\">[rc:step-mass]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-230.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rc-step",

For a ribbon of half-width $\\ell$, the same assertion has mesh cutoff $v_0\\ell$ and lower bound $c\\ell^d$ in <a href=\"#rc:step-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rc:step-mass\">[rc:step-mass]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-230.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-es-guide-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F_n:\\mathbb H\\to D_n$ be conformal, with inverse $g_n$, and let $\\alpha_n:[0,T]\\to\\overline{D_n}$ be $C$-Lipschitz guides satisfying $$\\begin{gathered}\n \\alpha_n(0)=a_n\\in\\partial D_n,\\qquad\n \\alpha_n(t)\\in D_n\\quad(t>0),\\\\\n \\operatorname{dist}(\\alpha_n(t),\\partial D_n)\n       \\ge c\\min(t,1).\n \\end{gathered}$$ Assume that $a_n$ stays in a bounded set and that $\\beta_n=g_n\\circ\\alpha_n$, initially defined for $t>0$, extends continuously to $\\beta_n(0)=0$. Fix $t_w\\in(0,T]$, put $w_n=\\alpha_n(t_w)$, and suppose that $$|g_n(w_n)|=1,\\qquad \\operatorname{Im}g_n(w_n)\\ge s>0.$$ There is a subsequence on which $\\alpha_n$ converges uniformly to a guide $\\alpha$, $F_n$ converges locally uniformly on $\\mathbb H$ to a nonconstant univalent map $F$, and $\\beta_n$ converges locally uniformly on $(0,T]$ to $\\beta$, where $F\\circ\\beta=\\alpha$. In addition, $$\\label{es:uniform-tip-collapse}\n \\lim_{h\\downarrow0}\\sup_n\\sup_{0\\le t\\le h}|\\beta_n(t)|=0.$$ Consequently $\\beta(0+)=0$. If $a_n\\to a$, then $a\\notin F(\\mathbb H)$ and $F(\\beta(t))\\to a$ as $t\\downarrow0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-230.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-es-guide-compactness",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-es-good-semicircle",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be univalent on $\\mathbb H$, and let $a\\notin F(\\mathbb H)$. Suppose $\\beta:[0,T]\\to\\overline{\\mathbb H}$ is continuous, $\\beta(0)=0$, $\\beta(t)\\in\\mathbb H$ for $t>0$, and $F(\\beta(t))\\to a$ as $t\\downarrow0$. Fix $r>0$ and $L>1$, and write $A=\\log L$. Choose $t_0>0$ so that $$|F(\\beta(t))-a|\\le r/L\\quad(0<t\\le t_0),\n \\qquad b_0=|\\beta(t_0)|>0.$$ In every unit logarithmic slab $(q,q+1)$ lying below $\\log b_0$, there is $B\\in(e^q,e^{q+1})$ such that, provided $A>2\\pi^2$, $$\\label{es:semicircle-bound}\n \\sup_{0<\\theta<\\pi}|F(Be^{i\\theta})-a|\n       \\le r\\exp\\{-A+\\pi\\sqrt{2A}\\}.$$ Moreover $$\\label{es:sector-bound}\n \\sup_{\\substack{B/8\\le|z|\\le B\\\\\n                 \\operatorname{Im}z\\ge |z|/2}}\n |F(z)-a|\n       \\le r\\exp\\{-A+\\pi\\sqrt{2A}+6\\}.$$ For $A\\ge64$ both upper bounds are smaller than $r/4$.",
        "source_id": "PM-230.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-es-good-semicircle",

Fix $r>0$ and $L>1$, and write $A=\\log L$. Choose $t_0>0$ so that $$|F(\\beta(t))-a|\\le r/L\\quad(0<t\\le t_0),\n \\qquad b_0=|\\beta(t_0)|>0.$$ In every unit logarithmic slab $(q,q+1)$ lying below $\\log b_0$, there is $B\\in(e^q,e^{q+1})$ such that, provided $A>2\\pi^2$, $$\\label{es:semicircle-bound}\n \\sup_{0<\\theta<\\pi}|F(Be^{i\\theta})-a|\n       \\le r\\exp\\{-A+\\pi\\sqrt{2A}\\}.$$ Moreover $$\\label{es:sector-bound}\n \\sup_{\\substack{B/8\\le|z|\\le B\\\\\n                 \\operatorname{Im}z\\ge |z|/2}}\n |F(z)-a|\n       \\le r\\exp\\{-A+\\pi\\sqrt{2A}+6\\}.$$ For $A\\ge64$ both upper bounds are smaller than $r/4$.",
          "source_id": "PM-230.2"

      "exact_statement": "Let $F$ be univalent on $\\mathbb H$, and let $a\\notin F(\\mathbb H)$.       "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-es-good-semicircle",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be univalent on $\\mathbb H$, and let $a\\notin F(\\mathbb H)$. Suppose $\\beta:[0,T]\\to\\overline{\\mathbb H}$ is continuous, $\\beta(0)=0$, $\\beta(t)\\in\\mathbb H$ for $t>0$, and $F(\\beta(t))\\to a$ as $t\\downarrow0$. Fix $r>0$ and $L>1$, and write $A=\\log L$. Choose $t_0>0$ so that $$|F(\\beta(t))-a|\\le r/L\\quad(0<t\\le t_0),\n \\qquad b_0=|\\beta(t_0)|>0.$$ In every unit logarithmic slab $(q,q+1)$ lying below $\\log b_0$, there is $B\\in(e^q,e^{q+1})$ such that, provided $A>2\\pi^2$, $$\\label{es:semicircle-bound}\n \\sup_{0<\\theta<\\pi}|F(Be^{i\\theta})-a|\n       \\le r\\exp\\{-A+\\pi\\sqrt{2A}\\}.$$ Moreover $$\\label{es:sector-bound}\n \\sup_{\\substack{B/8\\le|z|\\le B\\\\\n                 \\operatorname{Im}z\\ge |z|/2}}\n |F(z)-a|\n       \\le r\\exp\\{-A+\\pi\\sqrt{2A}+6\\}.$$ For $A\\ge64$ both upper bounds are smaller than $r/4$.",
          "source_id": "PM-230.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-es-good-semicircle"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rc-dense",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $s_0>0$. There are constants $c,C,C_2>0$, $C_1>1$, and an integer $m_0$ with the following property. Let $S$ be a stopping time of ordinary chordal SLE in $\\mathbb H$, and let $T_z$ be the lifetime of an interior marked point $z$. The assertion below holds conditionally almost surely on $\\{S<\\infty,\\ S<T_z\\}$, in the surviving state at $S$ or its image under a translation and positive dilation, with the transformed marked point still denoted by $z$. Suppose the marked point in that state has conformal radius $r$ and angle sine at least $s_0$. Let $\\rho$ be the first subsequent live stop at which its conformal radius is $r/C_1$, with $\\rho=\\infty$ if that level is not reached, and use the finite centered law of Lemma <a href=\"#ll:finite-tilt\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ll:finite-tilt\">9</a> through $\\rho$. For each integer $m\\ge m_0$ there is a deterministic $\\delta_m>0$, independent of the stopped state, such that, for every prescribed deterministic physical mesh $0<v\\le r\\delta_m$, there is an $\\mathcal F_\\rho$-measurable success event with conditional centered probability at least $$\\label{rc:dense-probability}\n c\\exp(-Cm^2)$$ on which the experiment ends at a finite stopping time $T<\\rho$ and generates sausage mass at least $$\\label{rc:dense-mass}\n v^{-p}\\operatorname{Area}\n  \\bigl(N_v(\\gamma_{\\rm experiment})\\cap B(z,C_2r)\\bigr)\n       \\ge c r^d m^p.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-230.2"
      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Gauge** ([source](#eq:hausdorff-definition)): A *gauge* is a continuous nondecreasing function $h:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ with $h(0)=0$ and $h(r)>0$ for $r>0$.
- **Gauge Hausdorff measure** ([source](#eq:hausdorff-definition)): Its Hausdorff measure, in the diameter convention used here, is $$\label{eq:hausdorff-definition} \mathcal H^h(E)=\lim_{\delta\downarrow0}\inf\left\{ \sum_j h(\operatorname{diam}U_j): E\subset\bigcup_jU_j,\quad \operatorname{diam}U_j\le\delta\right\},$$ where covers are countable.
- **Dimension and complementary exponent** ([source](#eq:parameters)): Fix $0<\kappa<8$ and set $$\label{eq:parameters} d=1+\frac\kappa8,\qquad p=2-d=1-\frac\kappa8.$$
- **Regular exact gauge** ([source](#eq:gauge)): Define, at all sufficiently small radii, $$\label{eq:gauge} h(r)=r^d\bigl(\log\log(1/r)\bigr)^{p/2}.$$
- **Whole-trace sausage measure** ([source](#sec:dense-cover)): For $v>0$, define the random measure $$\mu^v(A)=v^{-p}\int_A {\bf1}_{\{\operatorname{dist}(w,\Gamma)<v\}}\,dA(w). \label{eq:cover-sausage}$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **geometry** [claim](#stmt-geom-restart-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-restart-support): Boundary extension, prime-end access, and pointwise slit support. -> Restarted support in open sets.
- **finite-laws** [claim](#stmt-ll-finite-tilt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ll-finite-tilt): Localized finite Green changes of law and exhaustion. -> Consistent finite-radius centered laws.
- **mesh-tail** [claim](#stmt-mesh-tail) / [proof](#mesh:section): Asynchronous Green brackets or disjoint-window concentration, according to the parameter regime. -> Uniform final-mesh integrated-density tails.
- **retained-mass** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#retain:section): Remove excessive dyadic squares, take a supported weak limit, and restart on deterministic slots. -> Positive gauge measure on all positive-time compact segments.
- **uniform-ribbon** [claim](#stmt-rc-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-es-good-semicircle-2): Contradiction compactness, localization, finite witness selection, and unconditional sausage transfer. -> State-uniform ribbon passage and mesh cutoff.
- **dense-visit** [claim](#stmt-rc-dense) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rc-dense): Iterate conditional ribbon steps in a serpentine array and apply a finite stopped tilt. -> Dense finite-mesh occupation.
- **cover** [claim](#stmt-prop-dense-cover-upper) / [proof](#sec:dense-cover): Use geometric test radii, adapted failure bounds, and greedy disjoint disks charged to a common sausage mesh. -> Finite expected gauge measure in bounded disks.
- **comparison** [claim](#stmt-cor-larger-gauge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-larger-gauge): Compare gauge ratios on fine covers. -> Non-sigma-finiteness for the larger gauge.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-main**: For each fixed parameter in the stated range, every nontrivial positive-time compact trace segment has positive finite gauge Hausdorff measure on a common probability-one event. The whole trace has finite expected gauge measure in every deterministic bounded disk.
- **I-tail**: The integrated stopped-density tail is uniform in the final distance and in permitted collared initial marked domains, including their conditional stopped pasts.
- **I-greedy**: Decreasing-radius greedy selection yields pairwise disjoint closed disks, and every discarded disk lies in the concentric triple of a selected disk.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-law**: Apply the gauge to an arbitrary sampled path or software trace. -> The positive finite measure conclusion has no stated guarantee for a different process law.
- **A-parameter**: Claim a common almost-sure event across all parameter values. -> A simultaneous parameter assertion exceeds the fixed-parameter theorem.
- **A-gauge**: Use the exponent-one iterated-logarithm gauge as finite trace length. -> Its restriction to positive-time compact trace segments is not sigma-finite.
- **A-length**: Identify the Hausdorff measure with natural parametrization or Minkowski content. -> The proof supplies no such identification.
- **A-computation**: Use the finite disk selector to certify the full random-curve theorem. -> Supplied disks do not establish sausage qualification or the limiting Hausdorff bounds.
- **A-independence**: Replace conditional success bounds with an independence assumption. -> Adaptive histories are not licensed to be independent by the argument.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The finite cover proof explicitly orders candidate disks by decreasing radius and discards intersecting disks. This implementation handles supplied finite rational disks only. It does not determine sausage-qualified candidates, the deterministic mesh cutoff, or the almost-sure Hausdorff measure.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of at most six disks [x,y,r], with rational strings.
# PRECONDITIONS: positive radii; index order is the fixed deterministic tie order.
# OUTPUTS: selected indices and an owner index for every input disk.
# TIME: O(n^2) exact rational operations; bit cost depends on input length.
# SPACE: O(n) rational records and indices.
# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpq, squared distances only, no floating point.
# FAILURE: malformed input or violated preconditions exits nonzero.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq
raw = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
if not isinstance(raw, list) or len(raw) > 6:
    raise ValueError('disk family bound')
disks = []
for row in raw:
    if not isinstance(row, list) or len(row) != 3 or any(not isinstance(a, str) for a in row):
        raise ValueError('rational triples required')
    x, y, radius = map(fmpq, row)
    if radius <= 0:
        raise ValueError('positive radii required')
    disks.append((x, y, radius))
order = sorted(range(len(disks)), key=lambda i: (-disks[i][2], i))
selected = []
owners = [-1] * len(disks)
for i in order:
    x, y, r = disks[i]
    for j in selected:
        u, v, s = disks[j]
        if (x-u)**2 + (y-v)**2 <= (r+s)**2:
            owners[i] = j
            break
    if owners[i] == -1:
        selected.append(i)
        owners[i] = i
print(json.dumps({'selected': selected, 'owners': owners}, sort_keys=True))
```

        "receipt_sha256": "231e9679a12e2127b17b9a64f1589126da28779d731998e92a71119e80c05288"
      }

    "oracle_code": "import json, sys, itertools\nfrom fractions import Fraction\nfamily = json.loads(open(sys.argv[1]).read())\nanswer = json.loads(open(sys.argv[2]).read())\nD = [tuple(Fraction(z) for z in disk) for disk in family]\nn = len(D)\nassert n <= 6 and all(t[2] > 0 for t in D)\npriority = sorted(range(n), key=lambda q: (-D[q][2], q))\ndef separated(a, b):\n    dx = D[a][0] - D[b][0]\n    dy = D[a][1] - D[b][1]\n    return dx*dx + dy*dy > (D[a][2] + D[b][2])**2\n# Enumerate all independent subsets; lexicographic priority selects the greedy subset.\nfeasible = []\nfor bits in itertools.product((0, 1), repeat=n):\n    subset = [priority[k] for k in range(n) if bits[k]]\n    if all(separated(a,b) for a,b in itertools.combinations(subset,2)):\n        feasible.append((bits, subset))\nchosen = max(feasible, key=lambda pair: pair[0])[1]\nassert sys.argv[3] == json.dumps(chosen, separators=(',', ':'))\nassert set(answer) == {'selected', 'owners'}\nassert answer['selected'] == chosen\nmapping = answer['owners']\nassert len(mapping) == n and all(type(q) is int for q in mapping)\nassert all(separated(a,b) for a,b in itertools.combinations(chosen,2))\nfor a in range(n):\n    eligible = [b for b in chosen if priority.index(b) <= priority.index(a) and (a == b or not separated(a,b))]\n    assert eligible and mapping[a] == eligible[0]\n    b = mapping[a]\n    dx = D[a][0] - D[b][0]\n    dy = D[a][1] - D[b][1]\n    slack = 3*D[b][2] - D[a][2]\n    assert D[a][2] <= D[b][2] and slack >= 0 and dx*dx+dy*dy <= slack*slack\n",
    "output_contract": "Return selected input indices in decreasing-radius order and one selected owner for each disk. Selected closed disks are pairwise disjoint, and each disk is contained in its owner's concentric triple.",
    "reason": "The finite cover proof explicitly orders candidate disks by decreasing radius and discards intersecting disks. This implementation handles supplied finite rational disks only. It does not determine sausage-qualified candidates, the deterministic mesh cutoff, or the almost-sure Hausdorff measure.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of at most six disks [x,y,r], with rational strings.\n# PRECONDITIONS: positive radii; index order is the fixed deterministic tie order.\n# OUTPUTS: selected indices and an owner index for every input disk.\n# TIME: O(n^2) exact rational operations; bit cost depends on input length.\n# SPACE: O(n) rational records and indices.\n# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpq, squared distances only, no floating point.\n# FAILURE: malformed input or violated preconditions exits nonzero.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpq\nraw = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\nif not isinstance(raw, list) or len(raw) > 6:\n    raise ValueError('disk family bound')\ndisks = []\nfor row in raw:\n    if not isinstance(row, list) or len(row) != 3 or any(not isinstance(a, str) for a in row):\n        raise ValueError('rational triples required')\n    x, y, radius = map(fmpq, row)\n    if radius <= 0:\n        raise ValueError('positive radii required')\n    disks.append((x, y, radius))\norder = sorted(range(len(disks)), key=lambda i: (-disks[i][2], i))\nselected = []\nowners = [-1] * len(disks)\nfor i in order:\n    x, y, r = disks[i]\n    for j in selected:\n        u, v, s = disks[j]\n        if (x-u)**2 + (y-v)**2 <= (r+s)**2:\n            owners[i] = j\n            break\n    if owners[i] == -1:\n        selected.append(i)\n        owners[i] = i\nprint(json.dumps({'selected': selected, 'owners': owners}, sort_keys=True))\n",

This supports a bounded geometric selection routine when the candidate family is already supplied. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (hypothesis): The positive finite measure conclusion applies to ordinary chordal SLE in the upper half-plane for each fixed parameter in the stated range. It does not assert a common event across different parameters. Blocked use: ['Applying the conclusion to an arbitrary random curve.', 'Claiming simultaneous validity over all parameters.']
- **L02** (edge_case): The simultaneous positivity statement concerns nontrivial compact segments at strictly positive times. Endpoint and degenerate segments require separate justification. Blocked use: ['Treating a singleton as a positive-measure segment.', 'Extending the stated positivity claim to a segment beginning at the initial time without justification.']
- **L03** (hypothesis): The explicit gauge is defined at sufficiently small radii and completed continuously and nondecreasingly at larger radii. Its asymptotic formula alone is not a global measurement prescription. Blocked use: ['Evaluating the logarithmic expression at arbitrary radii.']
- **L04** (counterexample_scope): Non-sigma-finiteness concerns the specified comparison gauge on the stated SLE segments. It is not a result about arbitrary gauges or arbitrary geometric sets. Blocked use: ['Inferring that every larger gauge fails for every curve.']
- **L05** (unknown): The relation between the closed-form gauge and the companion exact gauge remains open. Equivalence or proportionality is not established. Blocked use: ['Replacing either gauge by the other on the assumption of equivalence.']
- **L06** (unknown): Identification of the gauge Hausdorff measure with natural parametrization or Minkowski content is not supplied by the argument. Blocked use: ['Interpreting the measure as a calibrated natural-time estimator.']
- **L07** (hypothesis): Pointwise slit support provides a positive probability for an individual slit and tube. Uniform probability over rough pasts requires the separate uniform passage argument. Blocked use: ['Replacing a uniform conditional passage bound by individual support probabilities.']
- **L08** (formal_scope): The Green change of law is constructed through finite stopped paths. The argument does not provide a density on a completed infinite-future sigma field. Blocked use: ['Applying a finite-stop density identity directly at an infinite-future endpoint.']
- **L09** (hypothesis): The swallowing estimate and conditional physical-disk rule require the stated parameter regime. They cannot be used as stated throughout the full range of the main theorem. Blocked use: ['Using the physical-disk rule outside its parameter hypotheses.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): Uniform mesh tails require the stated collared surviving-domain class. Uniformity does not discard the geometric hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Applying the tail estimate in a domain without the required free collar.']
- **L11** (nonconstructive): The retained measure is obtained by a supported weak subsequential limit. This existence argument does not prescribe a computable choice of a limiting measure. Blocked use: ['Claiming a finite procedure that computes the exact Hausdorff measure from the weak-limit argument.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): The sausage lower-tail statement is for deterministic intervals. Random passage use proceeds through selected deterministic intervals and an unconditional exceptional event. Blocked use: ['Conditioning the sausage inequality on an arbitrary random passage interval.']
- **L13** (approximation): The covering charge uses one common finite sausage mesh. The charge bound does not assert convergence of these sausage measures to the gauge Hausdorff measure. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite sausage integral as an exact Hausdorff measure.']
- **L14** (formal_scope): The finite greedy selection can be implemented for supplied disks, but this local construction does not compute the SLE law, the qualifying sausage masses, or the exact Hausdorff measure. Blocked use: ['Interpreting a finite disk-selection implementation as a certificate of the full stochastic theorem.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 230 (`lean/docs/230.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [An exact Hausdorff gauge for SLE: A moment-integral and finite-batch construction](../../preprints/An-exact-Hausdorff-gauge-for-SLE-September-25-2026/An-exact-Hausdorff-gauge-for-SLE-September-25-2026.pdf)
> - [An explicit exact Hausdorff gauge for SLE: A regular formula from quantitative tails and dense visits](../../preprints/An-explicit-exact-Hausdorff-gauge-for-SLE-September-26-2026/An-explicit-exact-Hausdorff-gauge-for-SLE-September-26-2026.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> For $0<\kappa<8$, put $d=1+\kappa/8$. The formalization proves that every continuous nondecreasing Hausdorff gauge agreeing with $h(r)=r^d(\log\log(1/r))^{(2-d)/2}$ at sufficiently small positive radii almost surely assigns positive measure to every nontrivial compact positive-time segment of ordinary chordal $\mathrm{SLE}_\kappa$.

Comparator source `SLELowerPositivity.lean` (3398 bytes, sha256 `f550800b9a60db513d4c9a2a85f710d0036a6a9b8f717ee0e64c38edd35f606a`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! Positivity of the explicit Hausdorff gauge on all positive-time SLE segments. -/

noncomputable section
open Set Filter MeasureTheory
open scoped Topology ENNReal NNReal
namespace SLEExactGauge
universe v_lower

noncomputable def dimension (κ : ℝ) : ℝ := 1 + κ / 8

noncomputable def codimension (κ : ℝ) : ℝ := ((2 : ℕ) : ℝ) - dimension κ

def upperHalfPlane : Set ℂ := {z | 0 < z.im}

def survivingDomain (γ : ℝ≥0 → ℂ) (t : ℝ≥0) : Set ℂ :=
  {z | z ∈ upperHalfPlane \ (γ '' Icc 0 t) ∧
    ¬ Bornology.IsBounded (connectedComponentIn (upperHalfPlane \ (γ '' Icc 0 t)) z)}

def IsCapacityTwoWitness (U : ℝ≥0 → ℝ) (γ : ℝ≥0 → ℂ)
    (G F : ℝ≥0 → ℂ → ℂ) : Prop :=
  Continuous γ ∧ γ 0 = 0 ∧ (∀ t, 0 ≤ (γ t).im) ∧
    (∀ t, DifferentiableOn ℂ (G t) (survivingDomain γ t)) ∧
    (∀ t, DifferentiableOn ℂ (F t) upperHalfPlane) ∧
    (∀ t, MapsTo (G t) (survivingDomain γ t) upperHalfPlane) ∧
    (∀ t, MapsTo (F t) upperHalfPlane (survivingDomain γ t)) ∧
    (∀ t z, z ∈ survivingDomain γ t → F t (G t z) = z) ∧
    (∀ t z, z ∈ upperHalfPlane → G t (F t z) = z) ∧
    (∀ z ∈ upperHalfPlane, G 0 z = z) ∧
    (∀ t z, z ∈ survivingDomain γ t →
      HasDerivWithinAt (fun u : ℝ => G (Real.toNNReal u) z)
        (2 / (G t z - (U t : ℂ))) (Ici 0) (t : ℝ)) ∧
    (∀ t, Tendsto (fun z : ℂ => z * (G t z - z))
      (cocompact ℂ ⊓ 𝓟 (survivingDomain γ t)) (𝓝 (2 * (t : ℂ)))) ∧
    (∀ t, Tendsto (fun y : ℝ => F t ((U t : ℂ) + (y : ℂ) * Complex.I))
      (𝓝[>] 0) (𝓝 (γ t)))

def IsCapacityTwoTrace (U : ℝ≥0 → ℝ) (γ : ℝ≥0 → ℂ) : Prop :=
  ∃ G F : ℝ≥0 → ℂ → ℂ, IsCapacityTwoWitness U γ G F

def IsOrdinaryChordalSLE {Ω : Type*} [MeasurableSpace Ω]
    (κ : ℝ) (γ : Ω → ℝ≥0 → ℂ) (P : Measure Ω) : Prop :=
  (∀ t, Measurable (fun ω => γ ω t)) ∧
  ∃ B : ℝ≥0 → Ω → ℝ, ProbabilityTheory.IsBrownianReal B P ∧
    ∀ᵐ ω ∂P, IsCapacityTwoTrace (fun t => Real.sqrt κ * B t ω) (γ ω)

structure IsGauge (h : ℝ → ℝ) : Prop where
  continuousOn : ContinuousOn h (Ici 0)
  monotoneOn : MonotoneOn h (Ici 0)
  zero : h 0 = 0
  positive : ∀ r, 0 < r → 0 < h r

noncomputable def hFormula (κ r : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  r ^ dimension κ * (Real.log (Real.log (1 / r))) ^ (codimension κ / ((2 : ℕ) : ℝ))

def HasSmallRadiusFormula (h f : ℝ → ℝ) : Prop :=
  h =ᶠ[𝓝[>] 0] f

noncomputable def extendedGauge (h : ℝ → ℝ) (r : ℝ≥0∞) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ENNReal.ofReal (h r.toReal)

noncomputable def hausdorffGauge (h : ℝ → ℝ) : Measure ℂ :=
  Measure.mkMetric (extendedGauge h)

def segment (γ : ℝ≥0 → ℂ) (s t : ℝ≥0) : Set ℂ := γ '' Icc s t

def SourceLowerMainTarget : Prop :=
  ∀ (Ω : Type v_lower) (_ : MeasurableSpace Ω) (P : Measure Ω), IsProbabilityMeasure P →
  ∀ κ : ℝ, 0 < κ → κ < ((8 : ℕ) : ℝ) →
  ∀ (γ : Ω → ℝ≥0 → ℂ), IsOrdinaryChordalSLE κ γ P →
  ∀ h : ℝ → ℝ, IsGauge h → HasSmallRadiusFormula h (hFormula κ) →
    ∀ᵐ ω ∂P, ∀ s t : ℝ≥0, 0 < s → s < t →
      0 < hausdorffGauge h (segment (γ ω) s t)

theorem sourceLowerMain_proved : SourceLowerMainTarget := by
  sorry

end SLEExactGauge

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For each fixed $`0\lt \kappa\lt 8`$, let $`d=1+\kappa/8`$. The gauge $`h(r)=r^d(\log\log(1/r))^{(2-d)/2}`$ at sufficiently small radii almost surely gives positive finite Hausdorff measure to every nontrivial positive-time compact segment of chordal SLE<sub><i>κ</i></sub>. This gives an explicit solution to Schramm's Hausdorff-measure problem in this parameter range. The entire trace has finite expected measure in every bounded disk. The exponent-one iterated-logarithm gauge suggested by Schramm is not sigma-finite on any such segment.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A random curve whose dimension lies strictly between one and two requires a finer notion of length than arc length. A dimension theorem specifies the critical power of a small covering diameter, but that power alone need not give a nonzero measure. For ordinary chordal $\mathrm{SLE}_\kappa$, $0<\kappa<8$, Beffara identified the dimension $d=1+\kappa/8$ (Beffara 2008, main Theorem) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[beffara2008](#ref-beffara2008)]; Rezaei proved that its $d$-dimensional Hausdorff measure is zero (Rezaei 2018, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[rezaei2018](#ref-rezaei2018)]. The remaining question concerns the correction to the critical power, not a further change of dimension.

<a id="eq:hausdorff-definition"></a>
A *gauge* is a continuous nondecreasing function $h:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ with $h(0)=0$ and $h(r)>0$ for $r>0$. Its Hausdorff measure, in the diameter convention used here, is $$\label{eq:hausdorff-definition}
 \mathcal H^h(E)=\lim_{\delta\downarrow0}\inf\left\{
 \sum_j h(\operatorname{diam}U_j):
 E\subset\bigcup_jU_j,\quad \operatorname{diam}U_j\le\delta\right\},$$ where covers are countable. We write $\mathcal H^h_\delta$ for the infimum at a fixed cutoff. We seek a deterministic gauge that gives positive finite measure to each nontrivial compact trace segment. Hausdorff measure optimizes over spatial covers; ordered partitions of a parametrized curve lead to a different notion of length.

Schramm asked for a sigma-finite Hausdorff measure of the SLE trace and suggested the following candidate (Schramm 2007, Problem 7.1 and the following discussion) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[schramm2007](#ref-schramm2007)]: $$r^d\log\log(1/r).$$ The article *An exact Hausdorff gauge for SLE: A moment-integral and finite-batch construction* already answered the existence question for each fixed $0<\kappa<8$. Its deterministic moment integrals specify coefficients, and finite irregular batches of radii specify a gauge through a full infimum (OpenAI 2026, equations (5.8) and (6.4)) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[openai2026exact](#ref-openai2026exact)]. That gauge gives positive finite measure simultaneously on positive-time compact segments and finite expected measure of the entire trace in bounded regions (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1 and Proposition 8.2) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[openai2026exact](#ref-openai2026exact)].

The present paper identifies a regular closed-form gauge and proves its two Hausdorff inequalities from quantitative estimates at finite scales. The stopped-law, mass, and covering arguments below do not import a proof result from the companion. The relation between the two exact gauges remains open. The regular formula also settles the proposed exponent-one correction negatively: its measure is not sigma-finite on any positive-time compact segment.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The result

<a id="eq:parameters"></a>
<a id="eq:gauge"></a>
Fix $0<\kappa<8$ and set $$\label{eq:parameters}
 d=1+\frac\kappa8,\qquad p=2-d=1-\frac\kappa8.$$ Thus $1<d<2$ and $0<p<1$. Define, at all sufficiently small radii, $$\label{eq:gauge}
 h(r)=r^d\bigl(\log\log(1/r)\bigr)^{p/2}.$$ Set $h(0)=0$ and complete it continuously and nondecreasingly at larger radii. The expression is increasing near zero, and its completion does not affect <a href="#eq:hausdorff-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hausdorff-definition">[eq:hausdorff-definition]</a>.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $\gamma$ be ordinary chordal $\mathrm{SLE}_\kappa$ from $0$ to $\infty$ in $\mathbb H$, parametrized by half-plane capacity $2t$, and set $\Gamma=\gamma([0,\infty))$. For each fixed $0<\kappa<8$, on one event of probability one, $$0<\mathcal H^h(\gamma([s,t]))<\infty
 \qquad\text{for every real }0<s<t<\infty.$$ For every deterministic $0<R<\infty$, $$\mathbb E\mathcal H^h(\Gamma\cap\overline B(0,R))
       \le C_{\kappa,R}<\infty.$$ In particular, on the same probability-one event, $$\mathcal H^h\bigl(\Gamma\cap([-m,m]+i[0,m])\bigr)<\infty
 \qquad(m=1,2,\ldots).$$*

</div>

The spatial expectation concerns the whole trace at once, including its real-axis points. It is not obtained by adding bounds over successive finite time intervals. The event is for the fixed value of $\kappa$.

<a id="stmt-cor-larger-gauge"></a>
<div id="cor:larger-gauge" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *Let $g(r)=r^d\log\log(1/r)$ at sufficiently small radii, with any continuous nondecreasing completion. On the event in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the restriction of $\mathcal H^g$ to every $\gamma([s,t])$, $0<s<t<\infty$, is not sigma-finite; in particular its total measure is infinite.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-larger-gauge"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-larger-gauge</summary>

*Proof.* The ratio $h(r)/g(r)$ tends to zero. For each $\varepsilon>0$, all sufficiently fine covers therefore give $\mathcal H^h(A)\le\varepsilon\mathcal H^g(A)$. Every set of finite $\mathcal H^g$-measure has zero $\mathcal H^h$-measure. A countable cover of the segment by such sets would contradict its positive $\mathcal H^h$-measure. ◻

</details>

Natural parametrization provides another notion of SLE length. Lawler–Sheffield constructed it for $0<\kappa<4(7-\sqrt{33})$ (Lawler and Sheffield 2011, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[lawlersheffield2011](#ref-lawlersheffield2011)]; Lawler–Zhou extended the construction to $0<\kappa<8$ (Lawler and Zhou 2013, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[lawlerzhou2013](#ref-lawlerzhou2013)]; and Lawler–Rezaei identified it, up to normalization, with $d$-dimensional Minkowski content (Lawler and Rezaei 2015, Theorem 1.1 and p. 5) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[lawlerrezaei2015](#ref-lawlerrezaei2015)]. For chordal SLE in the half-plane, Holden–Yuan identify positive fine-mesh variation, up to a deterministic factor, with natural parametrization, including segments that meet the real boundary (Holden and Yuan 2026, Theorems 1.7–1.8) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[holdenyuan2026](#ref-holdenyuan2026)]. Their gauge $r^d(\log\log(1/r))^{-(d-1)}$ is applied to ordered partitions. The different logarithmic correction here belongs to arbitrary spatial covers. Our argument requires no identification of $\mathcal H^h$ with either natural parametrization or Minkowski content.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The two Hausdorff bounds

For the lower Hausdorff bound we need a positive measure on a piece of the trace whose mass in every small ball is at most a constant times $h(r)$. For the upper bound we need spatial covers whose total $h$-cost has a bounded expectation. Both tasks use finite-mesh quantities, but they use them in different ways.

First consider a disk $A$ with a fixed free collar in the surviving domain after a finite stopped past. This domain is the component toward infinity after filling the components cut off by the trace. At a live interior point $z$, the Green density is $R^{-p}\sin^{8/\kappa-1}\theta$, where $R$ is its conformal radius and $\theta$ is its argument in tip–target half-plane coordinates. Stop this density at the point’s own first live approach within distance $e$, and assign zero if no such approach occurs. Denote the resulting stopped weight by $W_e(z)$. The quantitative estimate is $$\mathbb P\left\{\int_AW_e(z)\,dA(z)>u\right\}
        \le C\exp(-cu^{2/p})$$ in unit-scale coordinates, for large $u$, uniformly in the permitted past and final mesh. This controls a fixed spatial scale while the approximation mesh decreases to zero. Rezaei and Zhan proved fixed-$n$ multipoint approach bounds and finiteness of moments of natural length (Rezaei and Zhan 2017, Theorems 1.1–1.2) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[rezaeizhan2017](#ref-rezaeizhan2017)]. Their estimates already allow arbitrary approach radii. The estimate here gives the quantitative $e^{-cu^{2/p}}$ tail for the integrated stopped density, with constants uniform over both the permitted pasts and the final meshes.

The exponent comes from testing a large mass first at an intermediate distance $\delta$. There the deterministic density bound gives mass of order $\delta^{-p}$. For each prescribed finer final mesh, the later increments raise this bound by only a fixed factor outside an event of probability at most $Ce^{-c\delta^{-2}}$, with constants independent of that final mesh. Taking $\delta$ of order $u^{-1/p}$, and using the pointwise cap for coarser meshes, produces the displayed tail. Section <a href="#mesh:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mesh:section">4</a> controls those increments in two ways. For $\kappa\le4$, Green-kernel decrease bounds the bracket of density increments that start at different times. For $\kappa>4$, spatially separated windows and a swallowing estimate control the surviving contributions.

For the lower bound, Section <a href="#retain:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="retain:section">5</a> deletes dyadic squares whose mass is too large. At scale $r=2^{-k}$ the threshold is $Dr^d(\log k)^{p/2}$ for a sufficiently large fixed constant $D$. Reaching the square costs $O(r^p)$, whereas its mass scale is $r^d$ and there are $O(r^{-2})$ squares. The powers cancel because $p+d=2$. The tail makes the remaining loss summable. Positive first moments and bounded second moments leave a positive retained mass with positive probability. A supported weak subsequential limit satisfies the required bound on every ball; restarting on deterministic capacity slots gives positivity on every positive-time interval.

The upper bound asks for the opposite kind of local event: a disk must occasionally contain *more* sausage area than its usual scale. For a curve segment $\eta$, its normalized sausage area at mesh $v$ is $$v^{-p}\operatorname{Area}\{z:\operatorname{dist}(z,\eta)<v\}.$$ Rezaei’s covering proof already combines dense occupation, conditional estimates after stopped histories, compactness, and prescribed spiral passages (Rezaei 2018, sec. 3, Lemmas 3.1–3.3) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[rezaei2018](#ref-rezaei2018)]. Using the same geometric idea, a serpentine ribbon with order $m$ strands provides order $m^2$ disjoint local tests at spatial scale $m^{-1}$. Successful tests contribute total normalized area of order $m^{2-d}=m^p$. If each step succeeds with a conditional probability bounded below uniformly over every admissible rough past, the whole visit costs at most an exponential in $m^2$: its probability is at least $ce^{-Cm^2}$. Pointwise support for a fixed smooth path, as in Tran–Yuan (Tran and Yuan 2020, Theorem 1.1, Corollary 1.2, and Proposition 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[tranyuan2020](#ref-tranyuan2020)], does not supply this common conditional probability over rough pasts.

Section <a href="#retain:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="retain:section">5</a> first proves an ordinary-law lower-tail statement for sausages on deterministic time intervals. In Section <a href="#rc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rc:section">6</a>, compactness of conformal maps along an interior guide and a uniform localization estimate turn pointwise support into one ribbon-step bound. The passage is described with fixed margins that persist under convergence of the maps. A finite selection of rational time intervals then transfers the sausage estimate, leaving a common positive probability, mass threshold, and deterministic mesh cutoff.

For the final cover take $m$ proportional to $\sqrt{\log n}$ and perform order $n$ tests at geometrically spaced radii $r_j$, with $j$ between $n/3$ and $2n/3$. Conditional success bounds, not independence, make total failure small. Since $\log n$ is comparable to $\log\log(1/r_j)$, the generated mass matches the correction in $h$. The deterministic mesh cutoff in the ribbon estimate lets every test use one physical mesh. Disjoint selected disks can then all be charged to the same whole-trace sausage measure.

Section <a href="#sec:dense-cover" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:dense-cover">7</a> carries out this cover. Its dense-occupation and multiscale strategy follows Rezaei (Rezaei 2018, sec. 3, especially equations (9)–(11)) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[rezaei2018](#ref-rezaei2018)], who selects maximal high-occupation circles and charges them using bounded overlap. Here a greedy family of disjoint disks is charged to one finite-mesh sausage measure, and the quantitative ribbon probability specifies the logarithmic gauge. To bound the cost of visited cells that the selected disks miss, the proof uses test failures visible at finite stops, where the finite Green changes of law apply. The disks themselves are selected afterward from the whole-trace sausage. The final charge has a bounded expectation in one fixed spatial disk, independently of time and height cutoffs. Increasing both cutoffs then yields the all-time assertion.
