```latex
\node[anchor=north,font=\small,align=center] at (10.25,5.97)
  {$\ell=h_0/(C_0m)\asymp m^{-1},\qquad N\asymp m^2$};

\end{scope}
\end{tikzpicture}
```

Visual witness description of PDF page 41 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
Two panels labeled (a) and (b):
- Panel (a), labeled "(a) Marked point and displaced disk": shows the upper half-plane $\mathbb{H}$ with horizontal boundary line $\mathbb{R}$ and origin $0$. On the vertical axis is marked point $w = g(z)$ on the circle $|w| = 1$. Displaced horizontally by distance $s_0/4$ is a disk of radius $s_0/20$ centered at point $w'$. Inside the disk is a vertical serpentine path. A curve labeled "smooth connector initially vertical" starts at $0$ and reaches the bottom of the serpentine path, while a vertical arrow labeled "future guide" exits upward from the top of the disk.
- Panel (b), labeled "(b) The array inside the disk (enlarged)": shows an enlarged view of the serpentine array containing $2m + 1$ vertical strands of length $2h_0$ and total width $2h_0$, connected by alternating semicircular turns at top and bottom. Short orange segments along the strands are labeled "disjoint middle regions".
Below the two panels is the formula: "$\ell = h_0/(C_0m) \asymp m^{-1}, \quad N \asymp m^2$".
```


Let $F$ be the inverse of the original stopped map. Choose a fixed $\chi>0$, depending only on $s_0$, so that the closed $\chi$-neighborhood of $\mathcal D$ is inside $B(w,y/2)$. Such a choice is uniform because the disk center is at distance $s_0/4$ from $w$ and its radius is $s_0/20$, while $y\ge s_0$. On this neighborhood, Koebe distortion gives $$c r\le |F'|\le C r,
 \qquad F(\hbox{that neighborhood})\subset B(z,C_2r).$$ Here $|F'(w)|=r/(2y)$ and $s_0\le y\le1$; all constants are uniform in the stopped domain. For the prescribed physical mesh $v$, put $$\eta=\lambda v/r,$$ where $\lambda>0$ is small enough for the upper Lipschitz comparison. Choose the deterministic $\delta_m>0$ so that $$\lambda\delta_m<\min\{v_0\ell,\chi/2\}.$$ Then $v\le r\delta_m$ makes the same parameter mesh $\eta$ admissible at every ribbon step. It also keeps the segment from any sausage point counted in $\mathcal D$ to a witnessing trace point inside the distortion neighborhood.

<a id="rc:layout-sum"></a>
Use vertical-slit support for the initial step from the real boundary, requiring confinement and centered arrival only. It has a fixed positive probability by scaling. At each subsequent first front crossing apply Lemma <a href="#rc:step" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rc:step">22</a> at this same prescribed mesh $\eta$. Impose its passage-and-mass event determined at that crossing, although only the mass contributions inside $\mathcal D$ will be counted. The auxiliary later suffix used to prove the lemma is not retained. Given previous successes, the stopped history is therefore admissible for the next step. Denote the intersection of these events by $E_\eta$. Iterated conditional probabilities give $$\mathbb P(E_\eta)\ge q_{\mathrm{init}}q^N
           \ge c\exp(-Cm^2).$$ On $E_\eta$, the disjoint middle strips inside the disk yield normalized sausage area at the chosen mesh $\eta$ of at least $$\label{rc:layout-sum}
 c m^2\ell^d\ge c m^{2-d}=c m^p.$$ Disjointness concerns the regions in which area is counted, so it continues to hold if a sausage also meets another piece of the curve.

On this $\eta$-dependent success event, the sausage comparison in <a href="#rc:compact-sausage-transfer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rc:compact-sausage-transfer">[rc:compact-sausage-transfer]</a> maps the area counted in <a href="#rc:layout-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rc:layout-sum">[rc:layout-sum]</a> into the physical $v$-sausage. Conformal area change multiplies normalized area by at least $c r^d$, so <a href="#rc:dense-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rc:dense-mass">[rc:dense-mass]</a> follows. The choice of $\delta_m$ above depends only on $m$ and the fixed constants, uniformly in the stopped state.

It remains to check the radius cutoff and the change of law. Through completion, the prescribed trace and its filling remain in a narrow neighborhood of the connector, array disk and short terminal extension. More precisely, choose a closed obstacle consisting of $\mathcal D$, a thin neighborhood of the simple connector, and a thin neighborhood of the terminal stub from the disk exit through the completion front. The stub has length at most $2\ell$. The connector and stub attach to disjoint small arcs of the disk, and their neighborhoods are otherwise disjoint. This obstacle has connected complement in $\mathbb H$, so every filled prefix hull of a trace contained in it is contained in it as well.

The point $w$ is separated from this set and the real boundary by a fixed positive distance. The connector is below $w$ until its final vertical portion, whose horizontal distance from $w$ is bounded below by a fixed multiple of $s_0$; the array and terminal extension have the same separation. Thus $w$ can move vertically to height $2$, then horizontally to either $x=3$ or $x=-3$, and then down to a small real interval about that coordinate, with uniformly positive clearance from the prescribed trace except at the real endpoint. Small adjustments of these polygonal routes give fixed-width neighborhoods. They also connect $w$ and a fixed ball around it to infinity, so these sets are in the surviving domain throughout the experiment. The two chosen real intervals remain unvisited and unswallowed; their Loewner coordinates stay on their respective sides of the driving point by continuity.

Brownian tracking along the two routes therefore gives a uniform lower bound for the two boundary-arc harmonic measures in every successful intermediate domain. Hence the marked-point angle sine stays at least $s_1>0$. Its conformal radius stays above $c_1>0$ in parameter coordinates. Conformal covariance therefore keeps its physical radius above $c_2r$. Choose $C_1>1/c_2$ with strict slack.

Let $T$ be the first crossing of the completion front. It is a stopping time and is finite on $E_\eta$. On $E_\eta$, the one-point density at this stop, $M=R^{-p}\sin^{a_0}\theta$, $a_0=8/\kappa-1$, satisfies $$\frac{M_T}{M_0}
 =\left(\frac{R_T}{r}\right)^{-p}
     \left(\frac{\sin\theta_T}{\sin\theta_0}\right)^{a_0}
 \ge s_1^{a_0}.$$ Stop the density at the radius cutoff and use the finite stopped change of law, first also stopping at a deterministic capacity horizon and then increasing that horizon. Since success occurs in finite time and before the radius cutoff, it follows that its tilted probability is at least $s_1^{a_0}$ times its ordinary probability. This proves <a href="#rc:dense-probability" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rc:dense-probability">[rc:dense-probability]</a> and <a href="#rc:dense-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rc:dense-mass">[rc:dense-mass]</a>. All tests concern the already generated sausage at finite stops; no limiting marked-point visit is used. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:dense-cover"></a>
### Covers charged to a single sausage mesh

We prove finite expected $h$-measure of the entire trace in each bounded region. Select disjoint disks carrying enough normalized sausage area, all measured at one deterministic mesh. Their enlarged disks have a uniform expected covering cost; the visited cells they miss have a cost that tends to zero. Keeping that bound independent of the time and interior cutoffs gives the whole-time spatial conclusion.

Use the parameters from <a href="#eq:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameters">[eq:parameters]</a> and the gauge from <a href="#eq:gauge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:gauge">[eq:gauge]</a>. Put $\Gamma=\gamma([0,\infty))$. For $v>0$, define the random measure <a id="eq:cover-sausage"></a>$$\mu^v(A)=v^{-p}\int_A
       {\bf1}_{\{\operatorname{dist}(w,\Gamma)<v\}}\,dA(w).
 \label{eq:cover-sausage}$$ The sausage is taken in the whole plane. We also use $\mu_t^v$, defined by replacing $\Gamma$ with $\gamma([0,t])$. In particular, $\mu_t^v\le\mu^v$.

<a id="pm-h-29"></a>
###### The stopped estimates used by the cover.

<a id="eq:cover-angle"></a>
<a id="eq:cover-one-point"></a>
<a id="eq:cover-finite-tilt"></a>
For an interior marked point $z$, let $R_t(z)$ be its conformal radius in the surviving domain, and let $$M_t(z)=R_t(z)^{-p}\sin^{a_0}\theta_t(z),\qquad
 M_0(z)=(2\operatorname{Im}z)^{-p}
        \left(\frac{\operatorname{Im}z}{|z|}\right)^{a_0}.$$ Let $\mathbb P_z^*$ denote the finite-scale tilted laws. Lemmas <a href="#ll:finite-tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ll:finite-tilt">9</a>, <a href="#ll:inverse-angle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ll:inverse-angle">10</a>, and <a href="#ll:approach" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ll:approach">11</a> give consistency at finite conformal-radius stops, conditional change of law, and the following two bounds. If $\widehat S$ is one unit of the clock $-\log R_t(z)$ after a stop $S$, then $$\mathbb E_z^*\!\left[
      \sin^{-a_0}\theta_{\widehat S}(z)\mid\mathcal F_S\right]\le C.
 \label{eq:cover-angle}$$ If $S$ is a stop at which $z$ is alive and $\rho\le e^{-1}R_S(z)$, then $$\mathbb P\{\tau_\rho(z)<\infty\mid\mathcal F_S\}
      \le C\rho^p M_S(z),
 \label{eq:cover-one-point}$$ where $\tau_\rho$ here denotes the subsequent first stop with conformal radius $\rho$. Under the tilted law every finite clock level is reached. The finite-stop identity takes the form $$\mathbb E\!\left[
       {\bf1}_{\{S<\infty\}}M_S(z)X\right]
        =M_0(z)\mathbb E_z^*[X]
 \label{eq:cover-finite-tilt}$$ for nonnegative variables $X$ measurable at a fixed conformal-radius stop $S$, with the killed density assigned value zero.

Proposition <a href="#rc:dense" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rc:dense">25</a> supplies the following precise uniformity. Fix a positive angle cutoff. There are constants $b,c,C,C_2>0$, $C_1>1$, and an integer $m_0$ such that, for every integer $m\ge m_0$ and every radius $r>0$, there is a deterministic $\varepsilon(r,m)=r\delta_m>0$ with this property. At any state with $R(z)=r$ and angle sine above the cutoff, and for every prescribed $0<v\le\varepsilon(r,m)$, the tilted conditional probability is at least $c\exp(-Cm^2)$ that, before the radius falls to $r/C_1$, the future trace generates in $B(z,C_2r)$ a $v$-sausage of normalized area at least $b r^d m^p$. The constants and the cutoff $\varepsilon(r,m)$ are uniform in the entire stopped state. The event is observable by that clock endpoint. This formulation permits a single deterministic physical mesh to be used at all the tests below.

We also use the elementary visit geometry associated with the surviving-domain convention. If $\overline B(z,Lr)\subset\mathbb H$ initially, with $L$ a sufficiently large absolute constant, then a visit to the square of side $r$ centered at $z$ requires a finite live stop at conformal radius $Lr$. Indeed, while $z$ survives, Koebe gives $R_t(z)\le4\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial D_t)$. If $z$ is swallowed without the trace first meeting $\overline B(z,2r)$, that ball is in the swallowed component and the future trace cannot enter it. Otherwise, just before the first such meeting the live radius has decreased to at most $8r$; continuity gives the earlier radius crossing, for example with $L=16$. The same argument shows that a trace visit within distance $v$ of $z$ forces a live radius crossing at $16v$, provided $\overline B(z,16v)\subset\mathbb H$ initially. These statements concern the trace, rather than the filled hull, and apply also when $\kappa>4$.

<a id="stmt-lem-cover-charge"></a>
<div id="lem:cover-charge" class="lemma">

**Lemma 26** (Uniform bounded-region charge). *For every $0<R<\infty$, there is $C_R<\infty$, depending only on $R$ and $\kappa$, such that $$\sup_{0<v\le1}\mathbb E\mu^v(B(0,R))\le C_R.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-cover-charge"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-cover-charge</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a fixed $A>8e$. If $y=\operatorname{Im}w>Av$, the visit geometry and <a href="#eq:cover-one-point" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-one-point">[eq:cover-one-point]</a>, with a fixed multiplicative slack in the target radius, give $$\mathbb P\{\operatorname{dist}(w,\Gamma)<v\}
       \le C v^p M_0(w).$$ For example, increasing $A$ if necessary makes the target $16v$ at least one clock unit below the initial radius $2y$. Since $\Gamma\subset\overline{\mathbb H}$, only the layer $-v<y\le Av$ contributes outside this range. Its area in $B(0,R)$ is at most $C_Rv$. Tonelli’s theorem therefore gives $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathbb E\mu^v(B(0,R))
 &\le C_Rv^{1-p}
       +C\int_{B(0,R)\cap\mathbb H}M_0(w)\,dA(w)\\
 &\le C_Rv^{1-p}
       +C\int_{-R}^{R}\int_0^R(2y)^{-p}\,dy\,dx
 \le C_R.
\end{aligned}$$ Here $p<1$ is exactly what makes the boundary integral finite. The estimate concerns the whole trace at all times. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-dense-cover-upper"></a>
<div id="prop:dense-cover-upper" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:cover-all-time-expectation"></a>
**Proposition 27** (The upper Hausdorff bound). *For every $0<R<\infty$, $$\mathbb E\mathcal H^h
       \bigl(\Gamma\cap\overline B(0,R)\bigr)\le C_R<\infty.
 \label{eq:cover-all-time-expectation}$$ Consequently, on one probability-one event, $$\mathcal H^h\bigl(\Gamma\cap([-m,m]+i[0,m])\bigr)<\infty
       \qquad(m=1,2,\ldots).$$ On that event every compact capacity-time segment has finite $\mathcal H^h$-measure.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-dense-cover-upper"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-dense-cover-upper</summary>

*Proof.* First fix a deterministic compact set $K\subset\mathbb H\cap\overline B(0,R)$. Choose $T_0>1+\log C_1$, and put $$r_j=e^{-T_0j},\qquad
 J_0=\lfloor n/3\rfloor,\qquad J_1=\lfloor2n/3\rfloor,
 \qquad m_n=\lfloor c_0\sqrt{\log n}\rfloor.$$ The constant $c_0>0$ will be chosen below. We omit finitely many initial $n$, so that $m_n\ge m_0$ and every radius tested is smaller than the initial conformal radius at the grid centers used.

<a id="eq:cover-mesh-choice"></a>
*One deterministic mesh.* Choose a deterministic sequence $v_n>0$ such that $v_n\to0$ and $$v_n\le\min_{J_0\le j\le J_1}
              \varepsilon(e^{-1}r_j,m_n).
 \label{eq:cover-mesh-choice}$$ For example, take half this finite minimum and also require $v_n\le2^{-n}$ and $v_n\le v_{n-1}/2$. This choice is deterministic because the dense-visit cutoff is uniform in the state. Write $\mu^{(n)}=\mu^{v_n}$.

Use the usual grid of closed squares of side $r_n$, and keep the finitely many squares meeting $K$. A center $z$ of such a square has initial height bounded below once $n$ is large. For the argument at this one center, use $\mathbb P_z^*$ only through the finite clock levels required. Let $S_j(z)$ be the first live stop with $R(z)=r_j$, and let $S'_j(z)$ be the stop one clock unit later, so that $R(z)=e^{-1}r_j$. All these stops are finite under $\mathbb P_z^*$.

<a id="eq:cover-adapted-success"></a>
By <a href="#eq:cover-angle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-angle">[eq:cover-angle]</a>, a fixed angle cutoff can be chosen so that the conditional probability of a good angle at $S'_j$, given the complete history through $S_j$, is at least $1/2$. The dense-visit input and <a href="#eq:cover-mesh-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-mesh-choice">[eq:cover-mesh-choice]</a> then give a conditional success probability at least $$c\exp(-Cm_n^2).$$ On success, the prescribed mass has been generated before $S_{j+1}$, since $T_0>1+\log C_1$. Choose a fixed $C_3\ge1$ so that all these mass tests lie in $$D_j(z)=\overline B(z,C_3r_j).$$ There is a constant $b_1>0$, independent of $n,j,z$, such that success implies $$\mu_{S_{j+1}(z)}^{v_n}(D_j(z))
     \ge b_1 r_j^d(\log n)^{p/2}.
 \label{eq:cover-adapted-success}$$ This uses $m_n\ge(c_0/2)\sqrt{\log n}$ for large $n$. Let $A_{n,j}(z)$ be the event that $S_{j+1}(z)<\infty$ and <a href="#eq:cover-adapted-success" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-adapted-success">[eq:cover-adapted-success]</a> holds. Under the tilted law all these endpoint stops are finite. The event $A_{n,j}(z)$ is measurable at $S_{j+1}(z)$, because its sausage uses only the trace through that stop. Choose $c_0$ so small that $Cc_0^2<1/2$. For all sufficiently large $n$, the dense-visit lower bound therefore gives $$\mathbb P_z^*\!\left(A_{n,j}(z)\mid\mathcal F_{S_j(z)}\right)
       \ge n^{-1/2}.$$

Put $$F_n(z)=\bigcap_{j=J_0}^{J_1}A_{n,j}(z)^c.$$ Every preceding test is observable when the next one starts. Iterated conditional expectations give <a id="eq:cover-failure"></a>$$\mathbb P_z^*(F_n(z))
 \le(1-n^{-1/2})^{J_1-J_0+1}
 \le C\exp(-c\sqrt n).
 \label{eq:cover-failure}$$ No independence is required between tests or between centers.

<a id="eq:cover-candidate"></a>
<a id="eq:cover-missed-cell"></a>
*Returning to the original law.* Call $D_j(z)$ a candidate if $$\mu^{(n)}(D_j(z))\ge b_1r_j^d(\log n)^{p/2}.
 \label{eq:cover-candidate}$$ This is a terminal-sausage test. A cell visited by the trace and having no candidate must satisfy the adapted failure event $F_n(z)$: at each finite endpoint, $\mu_{S_{j+1}}^{v_n}\le\mu^{(n)}$. By the visit geometry, the visit also requires the live stop $\tau_{Lr_n}(z)<\infty$, for the fixed constant $L$ above. For large $n$, that stop is after $S=S_{J_1+1}(z)$ with a gap of at least one clock unit. Thus on the event on the left below all the test endpoints are finite, and $F_n(z)$ is measurable at $S$. Applying <a href="#eq:cover-one-point" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-one-point">[eq:cover-one-point]</a>, followed by <a href="#eq:cover-finite-tilt" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-finite-tilt">[eq:cover-finite-tilt]</a>, gives $$\begin{aligned}
 &\mathbb P\{\text{the cell at }z\text{ is visited and has no candidate}\}
       \notag\\
 &\quad\le C r_n^p\,
       \mathbb E\!\left[
         {\bf1}_{\{S<\infty\}}M_S(z){\bf1}_{F_n(z)}\right]
       \notag\\
 &\quad= C r_n^p M_0(z)\mathbb P_z^*(F_n(z))
       \le C r_n^p M_0(z)e^{-c\sqrt n}.
 \label{eq:cover-missed-cell}
\end{aligned}$$ The full-sausage candidate information enters only through the inclusion in the adapted failure event. All changes of law occur at finite conformal-radius levels.

*The finite cover and its charge.* Order the finite collection of candidate disks by decreasing radius, using a fixed deterministic order to break ties. Select the first, discard all disks meeting it, and repeat. The selected closed disks are pairwise disjoint. A discarded disk has no greater radius than the selected disk meeting it, so it is contained in the concentric triple of that selected disk. For large $n$, every cell is contained in each of its candidate disks, since $r_n/r_{J_1}\to0$. Consequently the triples of the selected disks cover all cells having a candidate.

<a id="eq:cover-selected-charge"></a>
Uniformly for $J_0\le j\le J_1$, formula <a href="#eq:gauge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:gauge">[eq:gauge]</a> implies $$h(6C_3r_j)\le C r_j^d(\log n)^{p/2}.$$ The qualification <a href="#eq:cover-candidate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-candidate">[eq:cover-candidate]</a> and disjointness therefore show that the total cost of these triples is at most $$C\sum_{\text{selected }D}\mu^{(n)}(D)
       \le C\mu^{(n)}(B(0,R+1)).
 \label{eq:cover-selected-charge}$$ All the candidate disks lie in this fixed larger disk once $n$ is large. No limiting measure or convergence of sausages is needed in this estimate: every selected disk is charged to the same measure $\mu^{(n)}$.

<a id="eq:cover-cleanup"></a>
<a id="eq:cover-cost-expectation"></a>
Add every retained fine cell that is visited and has no candidate. These cells, together with the selected triples, form a finite cover of $\Gamma\cap K$. There are at most $C_Rr_n^{-2}$ retained cells; their centers stay a positive distance from the real line, so $M_0(z)\le C_K$. Since $h(\sqrt2r_n)\le Cr_n^d(\log n)^{p/2}$, <a href="#eq:cover-missed-cell" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-missed-cell">[eq:cover-missed-cell]</a> yields an expected added cost at most $$C_Kr_n^{-2}r_n^d(\log n)^{p/2}r_n^p e^{-c\sqrt n}
   =C_K(\log n)^{p/2}e^{-c\sqrt n}\longrightarrow0.
 \label{eq:cover-cleanup}$$ Here $d+p=2$. The maximal diameter of the cover is at most $$\delta_n=\max\{6C_3r_{J_0},\sqrt2r_n\}\longrightarrow0.$$ If $Z_n(K)$ is its total cost, Lemma <a href="#lem:cover-charge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cover-charge">26</a> and <a href="#eq:cover-selected-charge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-selected-charge">[eq:cover-selected-charge]</a>–<a href="#eq:cover-cleanup" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-cleanup">[eq:cover-cleanup]</a> show that $$\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb E Z_n(K)\le C_R.
 \label{eq:cover-cost-expectation}$$ The constant $C_R$ does not depend on the distance of $K$ from the real line. Only the vanishing error and the discarded initial values of $n$ may depend on $K$.

*Measurability and the Hausdorff limit.* For fixed $v$, the distance to the whole trace is the infimum over nonnegative rational times of $|w-\gamma(t)|$. Thus all the sausage integrals are measurable, by Tonelli’s theorem. There are finitely many candidate indicators and the greedy order is deterministic. A fixed closed cell meets the trace if and only if it meets $\gamma([0,N])$ for some integer $N$; each event is measurable by continuity of $\gamma$. It follows that $Z_n(K)$ is measurable.

For completeness, $\mathcal H^h(\gamma([0,T])\cap K)$ is also measurable for deterministic $T<\infty$ and compact $K$. In defining the Hausdorff limit of this random compact set, one may use finite covers whose members are finite unions of rational open balls. There are only countably many such covers. Their covering events are measurable: for an open union $U$, the event $\gamma([0,T])\cap K\subset U$ is the event that the compact trace avoids the deterministic compact set $K\setminus U$. Arbitrarily small open enlargements, using continuity of $h$, show that these countable covering infima have the same limit as the usual Hausdorff contents. This proves measurability.

<a id="eq:cover-compact-expectation"></a>
For every fixed $T$, the covers above cover $\gamma([0,T])\cap K$. Therefore, pathwise, $$\mathcal H^h(\gamma([0,T])\cap K)
       \le\liminf_{n\to\infty}Z_n(K).$$ Indeed every sufficiently late cover is admissible at any fixed positive diameter cutoff. Fatou’s lemma and <a href="#eq:cover-cost-expectation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-cost-expectation">[eq:cover-cost-expectation]</a> give $$\mathbb E\mathcal H^h(\gamma([0,T])\cap K)\le C_R,
 \label{eq:cover-compact-expectation}$$ uniformly in both $T$ and $K\subset\mathbb H\cap\overline B(0,R)$.

*The entire trace in a bounded region.* To retain this uniformity when approaching both infinite time and the real boundary, set $$K_q=\overline B(0,R)\cap\{\operatorname{Im}z\ge1/q\},
 \qquad E_q=\gamma([0,q])\cap K_q
       \quad(q=1,2,\ldots).$$ The sets $E_q$ are increasing compact sets, and $$\bigcup_{q\ge1}E_q=\Gamma\cap\overline B(0,R)\cap\mathbb H.$$ Gauge Hausdorff measure is a measure on Borel sets. Its continuity from below and then monotone convergence, applied to <a href="#eq:cover-compact-expectation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-compact-expectation">[eq:cover-compact-expectation]</a>, give $$\mathbb E\mathcal H^h
       (\Gamma\cap\overline B(0,R)\cap\mathbb H)
    =\lim_{q\to\infty}\mathbb E\mathcal H^h(E_q)
    \le C_R.$$ Finally, a bounded real interval is covered by $O_R(r^{-1})$ intervals of length $r$, whose total cost is $$O_R\!\left(r^{d-1}(\log\log(1/r))^{p/2}\right)\longrightarrow0,$$ because $d>1$. The real-axis part therefore has zero $\mathcal H^h$-measure. This proves <a href="#eq:cover-all-time-expectation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cover-all-time-expectation">[eq:cover-all-time-expectation]</a>.

Apply the result at integer radii and intersect the resulting countably many probability-one events. Every box in the statement is contained in one of these disks. Every compact time segment has bounded image by continuity of $\gamma$, so it too lies in such a disk. This proves both assertions simultaneously. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-dense-cover-upper-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-dense-cover-upper</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* Section <a href="#retain:positivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="retain:positivity">5.4</a> gives positivity simultaneously on all real $0<s<t<\infty$ for the fixed parameter $\kappa$. Proposition <a href="#prop:dense-cover-upper" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:dense-cover-upper">27</a> gives the all-time bounded-box estimate and finite measure on every compact time segment on another probability-one event. Their intersection proves all assertions. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-30"></a>

```json
  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "Forcing exit near an arbitrary boundary. (a,b) The first path segment crosses the rectangle $Q$ from bottom to top. It then follows an ordered, sufficiently thin corridor for one counterclockwise turn around $\\overline B(0,2)$ and returns across $Q$ from left to right. The two crossings intersect, creating a closed subpath with winding number one about the disk; its trace separates the disk from infinity. Self-intersections are allowed. Thus the path meets every connected unbounded complement that meets $\\overline B(0,1)$. (c) At each entrance $σ_{j}$ after the preceding block, a block of length $Ar^{2}$ begins. The orange pieces represent occupation of $E_{r} = {u ∈ D : dist (u,∂D) ≤ r}$; the gaps have none. Conditional on each block start before exit $ζ$, the probability of exit in that block is at least $η$. Hence the expected occupation is at most $Ar^{2}/η$.",
      "figure_id": "fig:brownian-circuit",
      "pdf_page": 21,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:brownian-circuit",
          "exact_quote": "\\BrownianCircuitCaption",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }

      "witness_description": "Three schematic panels labeled (a), (b), and (c):\n- Panel (a), labeled \"(a) A forced circuit after scaling $x = 0, r = 1$\": shows concentric dashed circles centered at 0, with the outer dashed circle labeled $\\overline{B}(0, 2)$ and the inner dashed circle of radius 1. A blue path labeled \"initial approach\" enters a vertical rectangle $Q$ from below, proceeds into an \"ordered corridor: one counterclockwise turn\" looping around $\\overline{B}(0, 2)$, and returns into $Q$ horizontally from the left.\n- Panel (b), labeled \"(b) The two crossings of $Q$\": shows an enlarged view of the rectangle $Q$ with a blue curve labeled \"first: bottom to top\" crossing vertically and a brown/orange curve labeled \"last: left to right\" crossing horizontally, intersecting at a point labeled \"a common point\".\n- Panel (c), labeled \"(c) Covering all occupation of $E_r$ by time blocks\": shows a horizontal timeline $t$ with times $\\sigma_1, \\sigma_2, \\sigma_3$ and a vertical dashed line labeled $\\zeta$. Blocks of duration $Ar^2$ begin at each $\\sigma_j$. Orange horizontal segments indicate \"visits to $E_r$\" separated by gaps labeled \"outside $E_r$\". A legend at the bottom right displays an orange bar labeled \"visits to $E_r$\".\nBelow the panels is the note: \"Schematic; spatial corridors and time blocks are not to scale.\""

      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,font=\\small,\n  line cap=round,line join=round,>=Latex]\n\\path[use as bounding box] (-.9,-1.05) rectangle (12.25,3.85);\n\\fill[ribbonblue!3] (0,0) rectangle (12,3);\n\\draw[black!40] (0,0)--(12,0) (0,3)--(12,3);\n\\fill[black!8] (0,1.15) rectangle (12,1.85);\n\\draw[black!35,dotted] (0,1.15)--(12,1.15) (0,1.85)--(12,1.85);\n\\fill[ribbonorange!20] (5.5,1.15) rectangle (7.5,1.85);\n\\draw[ribbonorange] (5.5,1.15) rectangle (7.5,1.85);\n\\draw[dashed,black!50] (3.5,-.2)--(3.5,3.3) (9.5,-.2)--(9.5,3.3);\n\\node[above] at (3.5,3.3) {front $s=k$};\n\\node[above] at (9.5,3.3) {$s=k+1$};\n\\draw[very thick] (0,1.4)--(.5,1.65)--(1,1.36)--(1.6,1.7)\n --(2.2,1.42)--(2.8,1.64)--(3.5,1.57);\n\\fill (3.5,1.57) circle (2pt);\n\\node[above left] at (3.5,1.7) {$a$};\n\\draw[thick,ribbonblue,->] (3.5,1.57)--(5,1.5)--(11.7,1.5);\n\\fill[white] (6.5,1.5) circle (2pt);\n\\draw[ribbonblue] (6.5,1.5) circle (2pt);\n\\node[below,align=center] at (6.5,.95) {waypoint\\\\$P(k+1/2,0)$};\n\\draw[thin,black!60] (6.5,.94)--(6.5,1.4);\n\\node[above,text=ribbonorange] at (6.5,2.25) {middle test region};\n\\draw[ribbonorange] (6.5,2.2)--(6.5,1.88);\n\\node[above,align=center] at (1.55,2.05) {past in the core\\\\$|x|<\\epsilon$};\n\\node[above,text=ribbonblue] at (10.65,1.85) {access guide $\\alpha$};\n\\node[left] at (0,3) {$x=1$};\n\\node[left] at (0,0) {$x=-1$};\n\\draw[<->] (3.5,-.65)--(9.5,-.65)\n node[midway,fill=white,inner sep=2pt] {one unit in $s$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A horizontal strip bounded above by $x = 1$ and below by $x = -1$. Two vertical dashed lines represent fronts at $s = k$ and $s = k + 1$, separated by a distance labeled \"one unit in s\". From the left, a black curve labeled \"past in the core $|x| < \\epsilon$\" reaches the line $s = k$ at a point labeled $a$. In the center between $s = k$ and $s = k + 1$ is an orange rectangular region labeled \"middle test region\", with an interior point labeled \"waypoint $P(k + 1/2, 0)$\". A blue curve labeled \"access guide $\\alpha$\" extends from $a$, traverses the middle test region through the waypoint, and exits past $s = k + 1$ to the right."
    },
    {
      "caption": "A dense visit through a narrow ribbon. (a) In normalized half-plane coordinates, the marked point $w$ is separated from a disk of radius $s_{0}/20$ centered at the horizontally displaced point $w′$. The connector starts vertically at $0$, reaches the first strand from below, and stays away from $w$; the final strand continues upward. (b) The disk contains $2m + 1$ strands of length $2h_{0}$ joined by alternating exterior semicircles. Choosing ribbon half-width $ℓ = h_{0}/(C_{0}m)$ gives order $m^{2}$ steps. The orange marks indicate selected disjoint middle test regions. Their sausage tests contribute order $m^{2}ℓ^{d} ≍ m^{2 − d} = m^{p}$. Both panels are schematic and not to scale; the array in (b) is enlarged, and the ribbon widths are exaggerated.",

      "witness_description": "Two panels labeled (a) and (b):\n- Panel (a), labeled \"(a) Marked point and displaced disk\": shows the upper half-plane $\\mathbb{H}$ with horizontal boundary line $\\mathbb{R}$ and origin $0$. On the vertical axis is marked point $w = g(z)$ on the circle $|w| = 1$. Displaced horizontally by distance $s_0/4$ is a disk of radius $s_0/20$ centered at point $w'$. Inside the disk is a vertical serpentine path. A curve labeled \"smooth connector initially vertical\" starts at $0$ and reaches the bottom of the serpentine path, while a vertical arrow labeled \"future guide\" exits upward from the top of the disk.\n- Panel (b), labeled \"(b) The array inside the disk (enlarged)\": shows an enlarged view of the serpentine array containing $2m + 1$ vertical strands of length $2h_0$ and total width $2h_0$, connected by alternating semicircular turns at top and bottom. Short orange segments along the strands are labeled \"disjoint middle regions\".\nBelow the two panels is the formula: \"$\\ell = h_0/(C_0m) \\asymp m^{-1}, \\quad N \\asymp m^2$\"."
```
